# library-graph 단계 기록

각 단계 job이 끝날 때 `- [x] <CODE> <날짜> — <확인 결과>` 한 줄을 붙인다. `python tools/graph_pipeline.py mark <CODE>`가 이 줄을 확인한다.

(승인 때 answers로 낮출 수 있음). 반론 reading("Beatrice는 만들어진 게 아니라 태어났다")은 Moreau로 바꿔도 참이라 대체 테스트 실패 → 내지 않음. 두 reading 대체 테스트(Moreau·Birth-Mark / Frankenstein·Moreau) 통과, quote 12개 모두 원문 1곳에서 찾아짐; catalog check 0 errors 0 warnings, test_catalog·build.py·--smoke 통과(drafts: 5 notes, 5 readings)

- [x] G1-10 2026-10-07 — 작가 노드: catalog.build가 ready 책 작가마다 kind person 노드(등록 person이면 그 id — 책의 등록 work 작가이거나 이름이 byline과 같을 때; 아니면 author:<slug>; name·byline·slug·works(오래된 순)·born·died(등록 person만), content/authors/<slug>.md가 있으면 첫 문단 about·doc)를 내고 author_links를 '책 → 작가'(199줄)로 바꿈; 작가 86명(등록 8: Shelley·Goethe·Wells·Hawthorne·Stevenson·Wilde·Poe·Bulwer-Lytton), 책 slug와 겹치는 작가 slug 없음. test_catalog에 두 빌드 작가 노드 수 = ready 책 서로 다른 작가 수(86)·책마다 작가 하나·works 일치·slug 유일 테스트 추가(통과). 그래프(3D·2D 공통): 작가 = 금박 메달(머리글자, 3D는 카메라를 보는 스프라이트로 책 위에 그려지고 포인터도 먼저 받음, 2D는 미리 그린 비트맵) + 늘 보이는 기울임 이름표; 책→작가 옅은 선(3D는 LineSegments 한 번에); 힘: 작가의 주 장르 책은 작가 쪽으로 강하게(0.5, 거리 14+메달), 다른 장르 책은 자기 장르에 남아 그 장르 안에서 작가별 작은 무리(heads 힘)로 모이고 선만 이어짐, 관계 책은 가운데 그대로 → 주 장르 책–작가 평균 거리 약 25(이웃 간격 24–34), 털뭉치 없음. 작가 hover → 작가와 그 책만 밝게(lit = 작가+책 확인)·툴팁(이름·생몰년·책 수); 클릭 → 작가 패널(오른쪽, 390px는 아래 시트: 메달·이름·생몰년·about(있으면)·In this library 책 목록(연도), 'About the author →'는 doc 있을 때만) + 카메라가 작가와 책들을 패널이 가리지 않는 쪽에 맞춤 + #/graph/<작가 slug>; 목록의 책 → 그 책으로 카메라·pull-out·Read; ×·Esc·배경 클릭으로 닫힘. 검색은 작가 이름도(작가가 그 책들보다 먼저), #/graph/<작가 slug> = 작가와 그 책들 포커스. 필터: 장르·연도 뒤 남은 책이 있는 작가만 남음(Gothic 18권 → 작가 10, 연도 13권 → 작가 8, 3D·2D 확인), 개수 표시 '199 books, 86 authors, 11 relations'. 책 패널의 Same author는 작가의 다른 책 전부. Key에 메달 항목. graph_qa.py --author <slug> 추가(메달 hover 툴팁·lit, 클릭 → 패널·포커스 URL·패널 밖 프레이밍, 첫 책 → pull-out → Read, 성 검색 → 작가 첫 결과): poe·hawthorne(3D 1280), poe(3D 390, 2D 1280), jane-austen·mary-shelley(2D) 통과; --g1 노드 수 기준 = ready 책 199 + 작가 86 = 285(일반·drafts), 터치 탭 검사는 책만 고름. 결과: --g1 3D·2D 모두 전부 통과(정지 60·팬 58–60·휠 59–61fps, 토글 날아가는 동안 55.6–58.9), --g2·--controls(1280·390·2D)·--node·--link(3D·2D 390)·--modes·--smoke·test_catalog·catalog check 통과. 고친 것: 작가 이름표 86개를 텍스처 하나씩 만들면 3D 책장→그래프 비행이 40fps로 떨어져 이름을 한 장 시트로, 책→작가 선 199개를 한 번에 그리고, 메달·이름·선은 비행 중 숨겼다가 착지 뒤 페이드인; 2D 메달은 프레임마다 그라디언트 대신 비트맵; 3D 이름표 겹침 계산을 64px 격자로; 390px pull-out에서 Same author 목록이 길어 Read가 화면 밖으로 나가던 것(.fi-links ul 높이 제한). sw v26. 남은 점: 토글 '날아가는 동안' fps는 이 기계에서 작가를 끈 기준선도 3회 중 1–2회 42–54로 빗나감(작가 켠 상태와 같은 범위); 전체 보기에서 작가 이름 86개가 겹쳐 Realism·Love 쪽이 빽빽함(가까이 가면 풀림); 3D 메달은 깊이와 상관없이 위에 그려짐; 스크린샷 logs/graph-shots/author-*.png, G1-1280-light·dark·390·390-dark.png(이번 --g1로 덮어씀)

2D·3D --g1 최종 실행은 통과); 전체 보기에서 작가 이름 86개가 서로·장르 이름과 겹치고 390에선 왼쪽 가장자리 이름이 잘림(dor Dostoevsky 등); 관계 책 무리가 작고 메달이 관계 선 위에 그려져 390 전체 보기에서 Dorian→Jekyll 선은 확대해야 누를 수 있음; 2D 390 터치 탭이 한 번 빗나감(재실행 2회 통과); - [x] G4-01 2026-10-07 — 책장⇄그래프 토글이 책 비행(FLIP)으로 바뀜: transit.js(job #1317이 쓰고 G1-09가 다듬음) — 화면 안 ready 책등을 한 번 재서 복제하고 transform·opacity로만 움직여 매 프레임 노드의 현재 화면 자리를 따라감(같은 작품 권들은 한 노드로 모임, 노드는 마지막 책이 내려앉을 때 보임), 돌아올 때는 노드에서 책장 자리로; prefers-reduced-motion이면 책이 날지 않고 두 화면이 크로스페이드. 이번에 고친 것: ① site/css/library.css에 .book-index 선택자만 지워지고 선언 3줄이 남아 @media (max-width: 860px) 블록이 통째로 무시되던 것을 지움 → 390px에서 책장이 화면 오른쪽 밖(left 392px)에 있어 책이 하나도 날지 않던 문제 해결(app.js의 죽은 .book-index 선택자도 뺌); ② 그래프로 갈 때 새 지도의 첫 무거운 프레임(셰이더·텍스처 업로드)이 비행과 겹치던 것을, 책이 살짝 뜬 채 빠른 프레임 두 번(최대 450ms)을 기다린 뒤 출발하게 함; ③ 날아가는 책등 글자의 opacity를 변할 때만 쓰고 합성 레이어로(will-change) — 매 프레임 책등을 다시 칠하던 것 제거. sw v24. graph_qa.py --toggle(3D·2D × 1280·390): 책 비행·모두 착지·reduced motion 크로스페이드 전부 통과; '날아가는 동안' fps는 그래프로 3D 55.6–60·2D 56.7–60(반복 측정 6회 중 대부분 58.9), 책장으로 51–60(평균 약 57) — 4 구성 중 1개꼴로 한쪽이 55 아래(41–54)로 빗나가고 재실행하면 통과; --smoke(3D·2D)·--modes·test_catalog 통과. 스크린샷 logs/graph-shots/toggle-mid-graph-1280/390.png·toggle-mid-shelves-*.png. 남은 점: 책장 착지 직후 화면 밖 달 섹션을 다시 그리는 200ms 프레임 하나(움직임이 끝난 뒤); 책장 쪽 비행 프레임 저하는 메인 스레드가 아니라 합성(책등 수백 개 레이어 + 사라지는 WebGL 층) 쪽이라 이번엔 더 줄이지 않음; 그래프 맵 해체 시점을 착지 뒤로 미루는 것은 A/B(8회씩)에서 차이 없어 넣지 않음
- [x] G4-02 2026-10-07 — 나란히 읽기: 새 라우트 #/pair/<reading>[/<n>](site/js/pair.js) — 선 팝오버의 reading마다 'Read side by side' 버튼(탭별), 리더 관계 카드에도 같은 링크(#/pair/birthmark-frank-1/2 → 2번째 쌍에서 열림 확인); 두 책이 좌우(720px 이하 위아래)로 열리고 alignment 구절마다 <mark>와 번호 배지·문단 막대, 현재 쌍은 진하게, 데스크톱은 가운데 홈에 쌍을 잇는 곡선, 아래 띠에 Same/Differs와 이전·다음 쌍 버튼, 'Open in the reader'는 현재 쌍 문단으로; 스크롤 따라가기 = 쌍마다 고정점(읽기선 38%)을 두고 그 둘레(반 화면, 쌍이 가까우면 간격의 1/3)는 1:1로 함께, 사이는 비례 이동, 멀리 떨어진 대응 구절(Rappaccini ¶132·134·135 → Frankenstein 2권·1권·2권)은 순간이동 대신 감속 추적, 웹 폰트로 본문 높이가 바뀌면 ResizeObserver로 다시 잼; 관계 색은 catalog.js relColour로 옮겨 그래프와 공유. graph_qa --pair 추가(팝오버 → 버튼 → 쌍 1이 두 쪽 읽기선 ±40px, 왼쪽 휠 → 오른쪽이 쌍 2로, 오른쪽 휠 → 왼쪽이 쌍 1로, 쌍 사이 단조·초과 없음, 이전/다음 버튼, 리더 링크, 콘솔 오류 0): moreau–frankenstein·rappaccini–frankenstein·dorian-gray–usher·jekyll–haunted 1280·390 모두 통과(맞춘 뒤 오차 0–1.5px); --smoke·--link moreau frankenstein·--g2(+--g1) 통과; 스크린샷 logs/graph-shots/G4-02-pair-*.png; sw v30(js/pair.js 추가)
- [x] G4-03 2026-10-07 — 타임라인 레이아웃: 그래프 오른쪽 아래 Web | Timeline 전환(선택은 localStorage sl.graph.layout에 남고 ?layout=timeline으로도 열림, 2D·3D 공통). 타임라인 = x축 출판 연도(1700년부터 1년 6.5단위, 그 전 연도 550 BC·850은 축 끊김 왼쪽 칸), y = 장르 레인(지도 영역 순서, 책 수에 따라 높이), 레인 안에서 책끼리 위아래로 비켜섬, 관계선은 항상 오래된 책이 왼쪽 → 입자·화살표가 왼쪽에서 오른쪽으로 흐름; 축: 레인 띠·10년 눈금선(50년 굵게)·연도·레인 이름, 화면 밖으로 나가면 연도는 아래, 레인 이름은 왼쪽 가장자리에 붙음(390px); 작가 이름은 타임라인에서 확대하거나 불이 켜질 때만; 3D는 정면을 보고 자동 회전 멈춤. 전환: 책마다 1.5초 easeInOut으로 미끄러짐(연도 순으로 왼→오 약간씩 늦게 출발), 축은 같이 페이드, 카메라도 함께 이동; 웹으로 돌아오면 떠나기 전 자리로(0.1–0.2단위 이내), 타임라인으로 열린 지도는 엔진 워밍업으로 만든 웹 자리를 첫 프레임 전에 저장해 같은 방식으로 돌아감(이전엔 힘을 풀어 놓아 100ms에 1000단위 튐); reduced motion이면 즉시. 성능: 타임라인 좌표·3D 축(라벨 한 장 시트, initTexture·셰이더 미리)은 지도가 들어오고 2.5초 뒤 idle에 미리 계산, 책이 모두 고정된 동안 collide 힘 건너뜀; 2D 글자를 화면 픽셀 고정 크기로 그림(줌마다 글자 크기가 바뀌어 글리프를 다시 그리던 것 → 2D 줌 애니메이션 39→58fps, 전환 28→56fps). 같이 고친 것: transit.js에서 노드가 화면 밖이라 제자리에서 사라진 책이 land를 안 불러 노드가 계속 숨던 것, 비행 중 떠나는 지도의 freeRect가 옛 root를 찾던 것(view 안에서 찾음). graph_qa --timeline 추가(+ 전역 --layout timeline): 3D·2D × 1280·390 모두 통과 — 199권 모두 이동, 프레임당 최대 4.1%(easing 최대값, 전환 자체 시계 기준), 50fps 이상(53–60), 관계선 11개 모두 왼→오·연도 역전 0, 3D 회전 없음, 다시 웹 = 제자리, 다음 방문에 타임라인 유지, reduced motion 즉시, 전환 버튼이 다른 컨트롤과 안 겹침. --layout timeline으로 --node·--link(1280·390·2D)·--author poe·--controls·--pair rappaccinis-daughter frankenstein·--toggle(3D·2D·390) 통과(3D 비행 fps 10회 중 9회 55 이상, 1회 54.5 = 알려진 흔들림); 웹에서 --smoke·--node·--link·--controls·--modes(빈 곳 클릭 자리를 전환 버튼 위로 옮김)·--author·--pair·--toggle 3/3·--g1(3D·2D)·test_catalog 통과. sw v31. 스크린샷 logs/graph-shots/G4-03-*.png(timeline·mid·web·grown × 3d/2d × 1280/390, 다크, Poe 확대)

Bulwer's ghost overpowers the narrator, who traces it to a living will and destroys its instruments; Wilde's Americans frighten the ghost instead, and he rests only when a child prays beside his chained bones." ② note Canterville answers Usher / r:canterville-usher-1(밤에 읽는 기사 로맨스 ¶43 'several books of ancient chivalry' ↔ ¶37 the 'Mad Trist'; 산 채로 가둔 가족 ¶62 'her brothers to starve me to death' ↔ ¶48 'We have put her living in the tomb!'; 집의 운명을 말하는 시 ¶86 'Then shall all the house be still' ↔ ¶24 'And laugh—but smile no more.') claim "Both tales put a family house under a doom in verse, with a kinsman shut alive in its walls and a romance of chivalry read at night. In Usher the romance comes true as the buried twin breaks out and the house falls; in Canterville the romance's cock will not crow again, and a child's prayer makes the house still." ③ note Dorian answers Canterville / r:dorian-canterville-1(돌아오는 붉은 얼룩 dorian-gray-2 ¶777 'why was the red stain larger' ↔ ¶37 'keep the blood-stain in proper repair'; 기도해 달라는 청 ¶221 'Pray, Dorian, pray' ↔ ¶88 'pray with me for my soul'; 평화를 바람 ¶779 'he would be at peace' ↔ ¶81 'to be at peace') claim "Both tales leave a murderer with a red stain that will not stay clean and a plea to pray. Sir Simon repaints his stain, asks a child to pray for him, and is forgiven into a quiet grave; Dorian's stain spreads of itself, he answers Basil's plea with a knife, and stabbing the picture for peace, he dies." contact 모두 none(Wilde의 Bulwer·Poe 독서 기록 없음 → leads; ③은 같은 작가지만 contact는 refers/read 근거로만 계산되어 none, leads에 적음). 대체 테스트: catalog substitute 목록(Usher / Dorian·Haunted·Jekyll / Birth-Mark·Usher·Frankenstein·Haunted·Moreau·Jekyll·Rappaccini) 모두 실패 → 통과(Birth-Mark의 지워지지 않는 붉은 자국은 살인·기도 청이 없어 실패). 내지 않은 후보: Canterville ↔ Jekyll(밤의 복도·변장 역할) — 대체 테스트에서 일반적이라 보류; context(Wilde 자신의 글)는 이번엔 없음. quote 9쌍 18개 + loci 1개 모두 원문 1곳; catalog check 0 errors 0 warnings, test_catalog·build.py·catalog build --drafts(14 notes, 14 readings)·--smoke 통과, --link canterville-ghost haunted-and-the-haunters / canterville-ghost fall-of-the-house-of-usher / dorian-gray canterville-ghost 팝오버 3쌍 → 리더 문단 이동 확인(logs/graph-shots/link-*-1280.png). site/ 변경 없음(sw 그대로)

The Prefect finds nothing, because the Minister made the real letter look like waste and left it in plain view." ② note Turn of the Screw answers Purloined Letter / r:turn-purloined-1(탁자에서 가져간 편지 ¶759 'from the table in the hall, you took, you know, my letter' ↔ ¶29 'he takes also from the table the letter to which he had no claim'; 편지의 값은 내용이 아님 ¶768 '“Nothing.”' ↔ ¶34 'this possession, and not any employment of the letter, which bestows the power'; 상대의 얼굴 읽기 ¶767 'the collapse of mockery…' ↔ ¶97 'I fashion the expression of my face…') claim "Both tales turn on a letter taken from a table to get a hold over its writer, and a questioner who reads the taker's mind. Dupin takes it back from the Minister unseen and leaves him to his ruin. The governess wrings from Miles that he took hers, found nothing in it and burned it, and her victory loses him." ③ note Jekyll answers Purloined Letter / r:jekyll-purloined-1(두 문서 대조 ¶148 'laid the two sheets of paper alongside' ↔ ¶116 'radically different from the one of which the Prefect had read us so minute a description'; 꾸민 필적 ¶128 'an odd, upright hand and signed “Edward Hyde”' ↔ ¶115 'in a diminutive female hand'; 한 사람이 둘 ¶155 '“Henry Jekyll forge for a murderer!”' ↔ ¶101 'he is both.') claim "Both tales set a reader to judge a letter by its hand, against a man who is two things at once. Dupin sees through a disguised address because he knows the Minister as both poet and mathematician. Guest finds Hyde's hand is Jekyll's differently sloped, and Utterson, unable to think them one man, concludes that Jekyll forged for a murderer." contact: Poe의 Austen 독서, James·Stevenson의 Poe 글은 우리 근거에 없어 none(leads, T0). 확인: catalog check 0 errors 0 warnings, test_catalog 통과, graph_qa --smoke 통과, catalog build --drafts(37 notes·36 readings·7 glosses), graph_qa --link turn-of-the-screw purloined-letter 툴팁·팝오버(3쌍)·구절 이동 통과. site/ 변경 없음.

습득물 광고로 범인 쪽을 부름 -1 ¶298 'a plain gold wedding ring, found in the roadway' ↔ ¶101 'may have the animal again, upon identifying it satisfactorily'; 공은 경찰이 가져감 -2 ¶319 'the credit of this smart capture belongs entirely to the well-known Scotland Yard officials' ↔ ¶129 'I am satisfied with having defeated him in his own castle') claim "Watson names Dupin and Holmes scorns him, yet Holmes works by Dupin's rules: strange details make a case easier, and a notice of a found thing brings the guilty party to the door. Dupin's notice brings the sailor, who confesses; Holmes's brings a disguised accomplice who escapes, and the police take the public credit." ② note Study in Scarlet answers Purloined Letter / r:scarlet-purloined-1(moderate, 3쌍: 확대경·줄자로 한 치씩 -1 ¶215 'he whipped a tape measure and a large round magnifying glass from his pocket' ↔ ¶56 'we scrutinized each individual square inch throughout the premises'; 지나친 위장이 의도를 드러냄 -1 ¶242 'but by a clumsy imitator who overdid his part' ↔ ¶116 'so suggestive of a design to delude the beholder'; 상대 입장에 서기 -1 ¶305 'Now put yourself in that man’s place.' ↔ ¶96 'an identification of the reasoner’s intellect with that of his opponent') claim "Both tales set a private reasoner beside police who search with lens and measure, against a culprit who disguises a sign to mislead. Poe gives the microscope to the failing Prefect, and Dupin wins by thinking as the Minister; Doyle gives the lens to Holmes, who sees through RACHE but, thinking as the killer, is fooled by his friend." ③ note Study in Scarlet answers Frankenstein / r:scarlet-frankenstein-1(moderate, 3쌍: 법이 벌하지 못함 -2 ¶261 'it was impossible for me to secure a conviction against them in any court' ↔ frankenstein-2 ¶333 'You refuse my just demand: I have but one resource'; 러시아까지 쫓음 -2 ¶281 'I have hunted you from Salt Lake City to St. Petersburg' ↔ ¶347 'Amidst the wilds of Tartary and Russia, although he still evaded me, I have ever followed in his track.'; 죽으며 일이 끝났는가 -2 ¶262 'I die knowing that my work in this world is done, and well done' ↔ ¶398 'I feel that I shall soon die, and he, my enemy and persecutor, may still be in being.') claim "Both novels end with an avenger who, getting no justice from the law, gives his life to hunting his enemy across continents and into Russia, and tells his story while dying. Victor never catches the Creature and dies with his enemy alive; Hope runs his men down in London, leaves their deaths to a choice of pills, and dies content." ④ note Yellow Wallpaper answers Study in Scarlet / r:yellow-scarlet-1(speculative — 접촉 근거 없이 이미지·행위 대응뿐이라 한 단계 낮춤, 3쌍: 벗겨진 벽지 ¶33 'It is stripped off—the paper—in great patches all around the head of my bed, about as far as I can reach' ↔ -1 ¶170 'here and there great strips had become detached and hung down, exposing the yellow plaster beneath'; 벽의 표시를 샅샅이 ¶96 'I will follow that pointless pattern to some sort of a conclusion' ↔ ¶215 'Finally, he examined with his glass the word upon the wall…'; 서성인 자국 ¶185 'a long, straight, even smooch, as if it had been rubbed over and over' ↔ ¶245 'I could read all that in the dust…') claim "Both tales fix the eye on a room whose wallpaper hangs in strips over yellow, and on marks showing who has been there. 사랑한 집안에서 몸 때문에 쫓겨남 -2 'he dashed me to the ground, and struck me violently with a stick.' ↔ -3 'because I had some rudiments of reason, added to the natural pravity of those animals, it was to be feared I might be able to seduce them') claim "Both books put a creature among beings he reveres, show him his own loathsome shape in still water, let him judge men's wars with disgust, and have his masters cast him out for what his body is. Swift's keepers are reasoning horses who work the man-brutes; Wells's keepers are man-brutes who eat the pretty men." ③ note Island of Doctor Moreau answers Gulliver's Travels(lead 밖에서 찾음 — '돌아와 사람을 견디지 못함'은 Moreau에 있음) / r:island-of-doctor-moreau-gullivers-travels-1(구해 준 선원들이 미쳤다고 여김 -2 'Neither the captain nor the mate would believe my story, judging that solitude and danger had made me mad' ↔ -3 'they all conjectured that my misfortunes had impaired my reason'; 고국 사람들이 섬의 짐승인간으로 보임 -2 'I could not persuade myself that the men and women I met were not also another Beast People' ↔ -3 'I considered them, as they really were, Yahoos in shape and disposition'; 사람을 피해 물러남 -2 'I have withdrawn myself from the confusion of cities and multitudes' ↔ -3 'The first money I laid out was to buy two young stone-horses, which I keep in a good stable') claim "Both books bring a castaway home from a land where beasts and men change places: his rescuers think him mad, and in England he sees his neighbours as that land's man-beasts and shuts himself away. Gulliver calls this the truth and lives with horses; Prendick fears it is his own disorder and turns to books and stars." 실패한 후보(leads에 기록): Time Machine의 '먼 사회를 본 여행자가 돌아와 사람을 견디지 못함' — 우리 원문의 Traveller는 돌아와 손님들과 저녁을 먹고 다시 떠날 뿐 사람을 꺼리는 장면이 없음 → 이 장면 쌍은 대체 테스트에서 Moreau로 감. 확인: catalog check 0 errors 0 warnings, test_catalog 통과, build·build --drafts(drafts 205 works·75 notes·74 readings·13 glosses), graph_qa --smoke 통과, graph_qa --link frankenstein·time-machine·island-of-doctor-moreau → gullivers-travels 모두 통과(툴팁·팝오버 3쌍·구절 이동). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로)

Ichabod cons Cotton Mather till dusk and flees what may be his rival in disguise; Bulwer's narrator carries Macaulay as an antidote, hunts the ghost, and finds a will far stranger than a prank." 실패한 후보: 없음(세 lead 모두 구절로 확인). 단 Rip의 Knickerbocker 액자(머리말·Note·Postscript)는 우리 Rip 원문이 아니라 context work:irving-rip-van-winkle-note에 있어, shares-form reading은 두 원문 안의 액자(허드슨 마을 열기·갈리는 판정·이야기꾼)만 맞춤 — Knickerbocker 이름 자체는 정렬하지 않음. 내지 않은 후보(leads에 기록): Northanger Abbey(믿기 쉬운 독자, 그러나 장난꾼·가짜 유령 없음), Hound of the Baskervilles(전설 뒤의 음모가 — heading 수준, 구절 대응 약함). 책 파이프라인 참고: 우리 sleepy-hollow.txt는 PG 41의 부제 'FOUND AMONG THE PAPERS OF THE LATE DIEDRICH KNICKERBOCKER.'를 빠뜨림(Postscript·'D. Swift's Houyhnhnms judge brutes and spare their lives; his projector judges his own countrymen and kills a hundred thousand a year." (쌍: 인구 감소가 목적 'the number of people will be thereby much lessened…one principal design' ↔ Houyhnhnm 회의의 거세 제안 'would in an age put an end to the whole species, without destroying life'; 가죽 신발 'admirable gloves for ladies, and summer boots for fine gentlemen' ↔ 'I supplied it with the skins of Yahoos dried in the sun'; 버려지는 바른 개혁 'Of teaching landlords to have at least one degree of mercy towards their tenants' ↔ Lagado 정치 projector들의 'wild, impossible chimeras'). 대체 테스트: Gulliver → Micromégas·Flatland·Moreau이면 거세·가죽·개혁 목록이 없어 거짓; MP → Time Machine이면 projector·개혁 목록이 없어 거짓 → 통과. ② note Time Machine answers Modest Proposal / r:time-machine-modest-proposal-1 — claim "Both books reason calmly to men who eat men: one class, ground down by rent, ends as fatted stock bred for another's table. Swift's rich are to eat the children of the poor, in a scheme never carried out; Wells's descendants of the workers eat the descendants of the rich, and the Traveller's calm breaks over Weena." (쌍: 'These Eloi were mere fatted cattle…probably saw to the breeding of.' ↔ 'twenty thousand may be reserved for breed…more than we allow to sheep, black cattle, or swine'; 지대가 생사를 가름 'they would no doubt have to pay rent…starve or be suffocated for arrears' ↔ 'very proper for landlords, who…have already devoured most of the parents'; 냉정한 관찰 'I tried to look at the thing in a scientific spirit.' ↔ 'dying, and rotting…as fast as can be reasonably expected'). 대체 테스트: MP → Gulliver이면 사람을 먹으려 기르는 장면·지대가 없어 거짓; Time Machine → War of the Worlds·Moreau이면 계급과 지대가 없어 거짓 → 통과. contact: ① 같은 작가(encounter 없어 none), ② 기록 없음. 실패·보류한 후보: lead의 '숫자로 무장한 이성' 쌍 중 Lilliput 수학자들이 사분의로 Gulliver의 식량을 '1724 of theirs'로 계산하는 장면은 잔인함 없는 계산이라 MP의 claim을 받치지 못해 쓰지 않음(Houyhnhnm 회의·Yahoo 가죽·Lagado로 대신함). Micromégas answers/← MP: 사람을 위에서 재기는 하나 사람을 쓰거나 줄이는 계획이 없고 남는 것(척도)은 대체 테스트에서 Gulliver로 감 → 거부. Moreau: 짐승을 사람으로 만드는 것은 사람을 가축으로 계산하는 것의 반대이고 대중에게 내놓는 계획이 없음; '사람을 가축처럼'은 Time Machine 쌍이 계급·지대와 함께 더 구체적으로 가짐 → 거부. cites: MP 본문의 Psalmanaazor·Laplanders·Topinamboo는 라이브러리 작품이 아님 → gloss 없음.

(쌍: 'The huge, melancholy expanse looked to him like a sea.' ↔ 'the Tappan Zee spread its dusky and indistinct waste of waters'; 유품 'a bundle of goose-quills, a notebook of white paper, three pairs of socks…' ↔ 'examined the bundle which contained all his worldly effects'; 'a spectre in a uniform like that of the chancellery officials' ↔ 'haunted by the ghost of the unfortunate pedagogue'). 대체 테스트: Sleepy Hollow → Canterville·Haunted and the Haunters(유령은 있으나 피해자가 유령이 되지 않음)·Rip Van Winkle(살아 돌아옴, 유품 목록 없음)·Young Goodman Brown(잔치·강도 없음)이면 거짓 → 통과. ② note Overcoat answers The Sorrows of Young Werther / r:overcoat-sorrows-of-young-werther-1 (case speculative) — claim "A man of the pen serves superiors who rule his wording, and rank shows him the door. (쌍: 'changing the first personal pronoun into the third' ↔ 'a deadly antipathy to all those transpositions of which I am so fond'; 'with whom you are speaking? Do you consider before whom you are standing?' ↔ 'I perceive the company is rather displeased at your being here.'; 'never been so scolded by a superior official' ↔ 'the minister gave me a reprimand,—a gentle one'). 대체 테스트: Werther → Northanger Abbey(큰 집에서 쫓겨나지만 서기가 아니고 문장을 심사받지 않음)·Gulliver(궁정·대신은 있으나 베끼는 하급자 없음)·Sleepy Hollow면 거짓 → 통과. contact: 둘 다 우리 원문에 Gogol의 독서 기록 없음 → none(leads). 실패·보류한 후보: lead Jekyll·Dorian(죽은 뒤 유령/분신으로 돌아옴) — 둘 다 죽은 뒤 돌아오지 않음(Hyde는 살아 있는 동안의 둘째 자아, 초상은 Dorian이 죽자 원래대로); Akaki의 유령은 분신이 아니라 죽은 본인이라 남는 공통점('사람보다 오래가는 형상')은 너무 일반적이고 대체 테스트에서 Canterville·Haunted로 감 → 거부. Canterville Ghost: 우스운 유령이 높은 사람을 놀라게 하나 가난·도둑맞음·계급·질책이 없음 → 거부. Bartleby·Notes from Underground는 뒤 단계(heading:copying-clerk만 마련). cites: 본문이 라이브러리 작품·작가를 부르지 않음 → gloss 없음.

Micromégas looks down from the stars and laughs; Candide suffers below, and a dervish says a sultan ignores the mice on his ship." (쌍: 'Observe, that the nose has been formed to bear spectacles—thus we have spectacles.' ↔ 'had all been made uniquely for mankind.'; Martin 'A million regimented assassins…for want of more honest employment.' ↔ '100,000 madmen of our species wearing hats, killing 100,000 other animals wearing turbans'; Dervish 'does he trouble his head whether the mice on board are at their ease or not?' ↔ 'he saw nothing but blank pages'). 대체 테스트: Micromégas → Gulliver(전쟁 판결은 있으나 목적인 박사·빈 대답 없음)·Flatland(Sphere가 더 높은 차원 이야기를 거절하나 설계 박사·전쟁 수 없음)·MP이면 거짓 → 통과. ③ note Candide answers A Modest Proposal / r:candide-modest-proposal-1 — claim "Both books take at its word the saying that the rich devour the poor: a respectable man advises eating people so the meat lasts, a child is sold for the parents' gain, and the eater sits at a European table. Swift only proposes it; in Candide it has been done, and the eaten tell it." (쌍: Iman 'exhorting them not to kill us all at once' ↔ 'twenty thousand may be reserved for breed'; Surinam 노예 'which is making the fortune of thy father and mother' ↔ 'the gain of eight shillings sterling per annum by the sale of their children'; 'This is the price at which you eat sugar in Europe.' ↔ 'landlords, who, as they have already devoured most of the parents'). 대체 테스트: MP → Time Machine(Morlock이 Eloi를 먹으나 권하는 사람·아이 판매·유럽의 식탁 없음)·Gulliver(Yahoo 가죽뿐, 먹지 않음)·Moby-Dick이면 거짓 → 통과. contact: ① 기록 없음(Micromégas가 Swift를 부르는 것은 1752년에 Swift를 안 증거일 뿐 Candide의 독서 기록이 아님 → leads), ② 같은 작가(encounter 없어 none), ③ 기록 없음. 실패·보류한 후보: lead ② Gulliver의 '여행으로 낙관을 무너뜨리는 풍자'는 Gulliver에 무너뜨릴 낙관론(best of worlds)이 없어 근거로 못 씀 → 대신 El Dorado ↔ Houyhnhnm(이성의 나라를 떠남)으로 note; Brobdingnag 왕의 'pernicious race of little odious vermin'은 이미 Micromégas→Gulliver reading이 씀, Candide의 Martin 쌍은 대체 테스트에서 Micromégas 쪽으로 가서 Gulliver note에 안 씀. Modest Proposal은 lead에 없지만 앞 단계(G10-01) 작품이고 구절 3쌍과 대체 테스트로 확인되어 note로 냄. Lisbon 시의 'thinking atoms…have measured the faint stars'·'the book of fate is closed to us'는 Micromégas와 겹치나 context 텍스트라 근거 아님 → context leads에만 적음.

Melville 'Hawthorne and His Mosses'가 'Scarlet Letter'를 이름으로 부르지만 그 paratext는 순서상 뒤 단계(G9-07)라 이 단계 그래프에 없음 → review context는 G9-07 보정 몫(leads에 적음). gloss 없음(본문의 Black Man·Mistress Hibbins·Governor Bellingham 등은 서고 작품 아님). note 3·reading 3(승인용, 모두 answers, contact none(같은 작가 — 계산상 none, leads), case moderate, 3쌍, 대체 테스트 begin 목록의 다른 이야기·장편 37편 모두 실패 → passed 기록, substitute가 낸 heading 동료 모두 tried에 포함): ① note Scarlet Letter answers Minister's Black Veil(lead 확인) / r:scarlet-letter-ministers-black-veil-1(죄가 주는 설교의 힘 'But this very burden it was that gave him sympathies so intimate with the sinful brotherhood of mankind' ↔ 'he became a man of awful power over souls that were in agony for sin.'; 죽어 가는 목사의 마지막 말 'He tells you, that, with all its mysterious horror, it is but the shadow of what he bears on his own breast' ↔ 'I look around me, and, lo! on every visage a Black Veil!'; 마음을 보이라 'Show freely to the world, if not your worst, yet some trait whereby the worst may be inferred!' ↔ 'When the friend shows his inmost heart to his friend; the lover to his best beloved') claim "Both tales make a minister's hidden sin the source of his power over sinners and end with his dying speech about it. Hooper keeps his veil and says every face hides a sin; Dimmesdale, who wore no sign, bares his own breast, and the moral asks for the showing Hooper said no one gives." ② note Scarlet Letter answers Birth-Mark(lead 확인) / r:scarlet-letter-birth-mark-1(더 깊이 파고드는 과학자 남편 'He now dug into the poor clergyman’s heart, like a miner searching for gold' ↔ 'Your case demands a remedy that shall go deeper.'; 약이 붉은 표지에 작용 'when old Roger Chillingworth, being a potent necromancer, had caused it to appear, through the agency of magic and poisonous drugs' ↔ 'The crimson hand, which at first had been strongly visible upon the marble paleness of Georgiana’s cheek, now grew more faintly outlined.'; 표지의 뜻 'the scarlet letter ceased to be a stigma which attracted the world’s scorn and bitterness, and became a type of something to be sorrowed over' ↔ 'It was the fatal flaw of humanity which Nature, in one shape or another, stamps ineffaceably on all her productions') claim "Both tales set a red mark of sin or flaw on a body and a husband of science, with laboratory and drugs, probing for it. Aylmer's draught fades his wife's mark and kills her; Chillingworth digs into his wife's lover, is said to raise a mark on his breast, and Hester's letter stays and changes meaning." ③ note Scarlet Letter answers Young Goodman Brown(lead 아님 — 숲의 Black Man과 숨은 죄의 앎으로 찾음, 구절로 확인) / r:scarlet-letter-young-goodman-brown-1(숲의 표지 'and they are to write their names with their own blood; and then he sets his mark on their bosoms.' ↔ 'Herein did the shape of evil dip his hand and prepare to lay the mark of baptism upon their foreheads'; 이웃의 숨은 죄를 아는 힘 'it gave her a sympathetic knowledge of the hidden sin in other hearts' ↔ 'This night it shall be granted you to know their secret deeds'; 숲에서 돌아와 만난 집사 'it was only by the most careful self-control that the former could refrain from uttering certain blasphemous suggestions that rose into his mind, respecting the communion-supper.' ↔ 'What God doth the wizard pray to?') claim "Both tales have a forest where the Black Man marks his own, a knowledge of pious neighbours' hidden sins, and a return to the village deacon. Brown believes the devil and sees guilt in every elder; Hester doubts what her letter shows her, and Dimmesdale, back from the forest, finds the evil in himself." 실패한 후보: 없음(두 lead 모두 구절로 확인). 내지 않은 후보: Rappaccini's Daughter(Chillingworth의 약초·'poisonous shrubs', 살아 있는 상징 Pearl — 과학자 남편은 Birth-Mark reading이 이미 다뤄 leads에만), Custom-House의 Old Manse 언급(paratext끼리는 context가 안 됨 → leads). 확인: catalog check --stage G9-05 0 errors 0 warnings, test_catalog --stage G9-05 통과, 모든 quote·locus가 우리 원문에서 정확히 한 곳.

(쌍: 'astonishing the Overworlders, to whom fire was a novelty' ↔ 일식 'with my arm stretched up pointing to the sun'; 'a thud—thud—thud, like the beating of some big engine' ↔ 'giving no sign of the rising hell in its bowels'; 'It had committed suicide.' ↔ 'a trap of our own making'). 대체 테스트: Yankee → Twenty Thousand Leagues(숨긴 기계는 있으나 세기를 건너거나 사람을 놀라게 하지 않음)·Gulliver·Rip·Machine Stops이면 거짓 → 통과. ③ note Connecticut Yankee answers Candide / r:connecticut-yankee-candide-1 — claim "Both books chain a man of rank as a slave, set a crowd at holiday round a legal killing, and end with the hero's company walled in a garden. (쌍: 'but I shall catch the most of them, if I know Hadleyburg nature.' ↔ 'The deacons of many a church have drunk the communion wine with me…'; 'the weakest of all weak things is a virtue which has not been tested in the fire.' ↔ 'Evil is the nature of mankind.'; 'the dying man passed away without knowing that once more he had done poor Burgess a wrong.' ↔ 'they carved no hopeful verse upon his tombstone, for his dying hour was gloom.'). 대체 테스트: YGB → Minister's Black Veil(모든 얼굴의 베일은 있으나 낯선 이·이름 불린 폭로 없음)·Scarlet Letter(숨은 죄인 한 사람)·Jekyll·Dorian·Usher이면 거짓 → 통과. ③ note Hadleyburg answers The Scarlet Letter / r:hadleyburg-scarlet-letter-1 — claim "Both tales end with a man the town reveres as pure, tortured by its praise, confessing before witnesses as he dies that he is as guilty as those it scorned. Dimmesdale's confession frees him, though some deny it; Richards confesses truly but wrongs Burgess with his last breath." (쌍: 'The stabs, through their very generosity, are so deep…' ↔ -1 'It is inconceivable, the agony with which this public veneration tortured him.'; 'I want you all to hear my confession, so that I may die a man, and not a dog.' ↔ -2 'behold me here, the one sinner of the world!'; 'the town’s pride in the purity of its one undiscredited important citizen began to dim down…' ↔ -2 'denied that there was any mark whatever on his breast…'). 대체 테스트: SL → Minister's Black Veil(Hooper는 베일 쓴 채 죽고 고백 없음)·YGB·Jekyll(고백이 사적 편지)·Bank-Note이면 거짓 → 통과. contact: ① 같은 작가(encounter 없어 none), ②③ 우리 자료에 Twain의 Hawthorne 독서 기록 없음 → none. 실패·보류한 후보: lead Modest Proposal — 맞는 쌍이 하나뿐(자기 이익을 부정하는 문서: MP 'I have no children, by which I can propose to get a single penny' ↔ 낯선 이의 'It is merely my way of testifying my gratitude'), 가난한 이를 가축처럼 셈하는 projector도 없어 heading:projectors-scheme 범위에도 안 맞음 → note 안 냄. Candide: Richards의 'it must be for the best'·'it was ordered. All things are'는 Pangloss와 겹치나 쌍이 하나뿐 → work leads. heading:hidden-sin에 index 안 함: 범위는 존경하던 이들의 죄를 알고 고립되는 한 사람인데 Hadleyburg에서는 온 마을이 폭로를 보며 웃음 → work leads.
- [x] G11-04 2026-10-09 — The Necklace 입고(모두 proposed), `catalog.py check --stage G11-04` 오류 0·경고 0, `test_catalog.py --stage G11-04` 통과. 새 person:guy-de-maupassant(LC n79069916 'Maupassant, Guy de, 1850-1893', 작가 문서에 person 줄 추가)·person:pol-neveux(LC n2021062087 'Neveux, Pol (Pol Louis), 1865-1939'). work:necklace(story, fr; title·author ev = PG 3090 'THE DIAMOND NECKLACE'·'Author: Guy de Maupassant'; first_pub 1884은 원문 근거 없음 → leads), text:necklace-pg3090(PG 3090 전집 번역, 이 단편의 역자 미상 → leads). 새 heading:borrowed-riches('Borrowed riches'). index enacts: necklace → borrowed-riches('Will you lend me this, only this?'), million-pound-bank-note → borrowed-riches('It is lent to you for thirty days, without interest.'). context: work:neveux-maupassant-study(Pol Neveux 'A Study', PG 3090 첫머리, 발췌: Maupassant의 해군성·공교육부 서기 시절) → necklace, role preface, content/contexts/neveux-maupassant-study.txt; 본문 locus 'a little clerk of the Ministry of Public Instruction'. 승인용 note·reading: ① note The Necklace answers The Overcoat / r:necklace-overcoat-1 (case moderate) — claim "A clerk's household stakes years of want on one fine thing, shows it off one night at a superior's party and loses it going home. Akaki's own cloak is paid for by hunger beforehand, and its loss kills him; Mathilde's necklace is borrowed, and the hunger comes after: ten years for a copy of a fake." (쌍: 'She no longer had the necklace around her neck!' ↔ 'Robbed of his cloak, and feeling frozen to the marrow'; 'late at night he often copied manuscript for five sous a page' ↔ 'copy his documents in his landlady's room, in order to economise the fuel in his own'; 'Why, my necklace was paste!' ↔ 'chose the best catskin in the shop, which was a very good imitation of the former'). 대체 테스트: Overcoat → Bank-Note(빌린 값진 것은 있으나 잃지 않음)·Notes from Underground(빌린 돈으로 비버 깃, 잃음 없음)면 거짓 → 통과. ② note The £1,000,000 Bank-Note answers The Necklace / r:million-pound-bank-note-necklace-1 (case moderate; Bank-Note 1893이 뒤라 note는 Bank-Note에서 나감) — claim "A poor person is lent, for a set time, a thing of great worth whose look admits them among the rich, and runs into debt on its account. Twain's note is real, unspent and handed back whole, and makes a fortune; Maupassant's necklace is paste and lost, and costs ten years of real francs." (쌍: 'It is lent to you for thirty days, without interest.' ↔ 'Will you lend me this, only this?'; 'Harris was so grateful that he forced loans upon me, and would not be denied' ↔ 'He did borrow, asking a thousand francs of one, five hundred of another, five louis here, three louis there.'; 'Have you the million-pound note?' ↔ 'You should have returned it sooner; I might have needed it.'). 대체 테스트: Necklace → Overcoat(산 것이지 빌린 것 아님)·Candide·Connecticut Yankee(빌림 없음)·Notes from Underground(돈은 빌리나 값진 물건은 아님)면 거짓 → 통과. contact: 우리 원문에 Maupassant가 Gogol을, Twain이 Maupassant를 읽은 기록 없음 → none(leads). 실패·보류한 후보: lead Overcoat는 ①, lead Bank-Note는 ②로 확인. Notes from Underground(빌린 돈으로 신분 위로 차려입기): 공통점이 대체 테스트에서 Bank-Note(Adams도 외상으로 옷)·Overcoat(Akaki도 털깃)로 감 → 너무 일반적, 거부. heading copying-clerk: Loisel이 밤에 필사하나 이야기는 Mathilde의 것이고 사무실이 안 나옴 → 색인 안 함. cites: 본문이 라이브러리 작품·작가를 부르지 않음 → gloss 없음.

Koteliansky·J. M. Murry 역, Boston: John W. Luce 1915, PG 55283 표제지). person:anton-chekhov는 G11-05에서 이미 있음. index enacts: the-bet → heading:money-as-test('Gentlemen, I stake two millions.'). context: 없음(PG 55283의 역자 주는 다른 단편의 러시아 이름 설명뿐). 승인용 note·reading: ① note The Bet answers Notes from Underground / r:the-bet-notes-from-underground-1 (case moderate) — claim "Two men cut off from people for years write out what books have made of them. Dostoevsky's clerk says we all prefer life in books and would be lost without them, and stays dreaming in his hole; Chekhov's lawyer, after fifteen years of nothing but books, despises books and life alike and walks out." (쌍: 'And I despise your books, despise all wordly blessings and wisdom.' ↔ 'we are all privately agreed that it is better in books'; 'In your books I cast myself into bottomless abysses … conquered whole countries' ↔ 'preaching new ideas and fighting a victorious Austerlitz against the obscurantists'; 'like the mice underground' ↔ 'creep ignominiously into its mouse-hole. There in its nasty, stinking, underground home' — 쥐 비유는 두 영역본(Garnett; Koteliansky·Murry)의 일치라 러시아어 원문에도 있는지는 leads). 대체 테스트: NfU → Bartleby(갇혀 있으나 읽지도 꿈꾸지도 않음)·Yellow Wallpaper(갇혀 쓰지만 책을 빼앗김)·Jekyll·Dorian·Overcoat·Rip이면 거짓 → 통과. ② note The Bet answers Bartleby / r:the-bet-bartleby-1 (case moderate) — claim "A man of business keeps a silent man shut in his premises, is tempted alone to kill him, and turns aside. Melville's lawyer is saved by a commandment and never learns why Bartleby refuses money and work; Chekhov's banker is stopped by the prisoner's written reasons for refusing two millions, and weeps." (쌍: 'smother him a moment with the pillow' ↔ 'the tragedy of the unfortunate Adams and the still more unfortunate Colt'; 'kissed the head of the strange man, and began to weep' ↔ 'A new commandment give I unto you, that ye love one another.'; 'I waive the two millions' ↔ 'you have not even touched that money yet'). 대체 테스트: Bartleby → Overcoat·Vanka·NfU·Seven Gables(살해 유혹을 사랑으로 물리치는 고용주·손대지 않은 돈 없음)면 거짓 → 통과. ③ note The Million-Pound Bank-Note answers The Bet / r:million-pound-bank-note-the-bet-1 (case moderate; Bank-Note 1893이 뒤라 note는 Bank-Note에서 나감; task의 leads에 없던 후보로, 같은 heading:money-as-test에서 찾음) — claim "Rich men settle an argument by betting a great sum on whether a man can last a fixed term. Chekhov's lawyer stakes his own freedom, lasts fifteen years and waives the millions in contempt; Twain's stranger is staked without being asked, lasts thirty days, and comes out rich and married, the end Chekhov's banker dreaded." (쌍: 'Brother B said he would bet twenty thousand pounds that the man would live thirty days' ↔ 'You stake two millions, I my freedom'; 'I earned it by thirty days’ judicious use of that little loan you let me have.' ↔ 'I waive the two millions of which I once dreamed as of paradise'; 'For it gave me my Portia.' ↔ 'He will take away my last farthing, marry, enjoy life, gamble on the Exchange'). 대체 테스트: The Bet → 그래프의 다른 작품 어디에도 부자들이 한 사람이 정해진 기간을 버티는지에 거는 내기가 없음 → 통과. contact: 우리 원문에 Chekhov가 Dostoevsky·Melville을, Twain이 Chekhov를 읽은 기록 없음 → 셋 다 none. 실패·보류한 후보: lead Rip Van Winkle(15년 뒤 나옴) — The Bet은 죄수가 창문으로 나와 문 밖으로 사라지는 데서 끝나고, 자기를 모르는 사람들 사이로 돌아오는 장면(Rip과 heading:sleeper-wakes의 핵심)이 없음. 남는 공통점(긴 세월·덥수룩한 수염·늙어 보이는 남자)은 Seven Gables의 Clifford로 바꿔도 참 → 대체 테스트 실패, note 안 함. sleeper-wakes index도 같은 이유로 안 함. cites: 본문의 Byron·Shakespeare·New Testament는 라이브러리 작품이 아님 → gloss 없음.

(쌍: 'There stood, facing the open window, a comfortable, roomy armchair.' ↔ 'the windows are barred for little children'; 'A kind intention or a cruel intention made the act seem no less a crime' ↔ 'He is very careful and loving, and hardly lets me stir without special direction.'; 'Louise, open the door! (쌍: 'She did not stop to ask if it were or were not a monstrous joy that held her.' ↔ 'There was a drop of comfort, at least, in this intelligence.'; 'There would be no one to live for her during those coming years; she would live for herself.' ↔ 'he had got his neck out of the yoke of matrimony, and could go in and out whenever he pleased'; 'It was Brently Mallard who entered, a little travel-stained, composedly carrying his grip-sack and umbrella.' ↔ 'it’s twenty years since he went away from home with his gun, and never has been heard of since'). 대체 테스트: Rip → Scarlet Letter(죽은 줄 안 남편이 돌아오나 그 죽음을 반긴 이 없음)·Werther·Sleepy Hollow·Yellow Wallpaper·Necklace면 거짓 → 통과. contact: 우리 원문에 Chopin이 Gilman·Irving을 읽은 기록 없음 → none(leads). 실패·보류한 후보: lead Yellow Wallpaper는 ①로 확인. Moreau's carved creatures learn a few words and slide back to the brute; Appin's cat, never cut, speaks perfectly, repeats what the gentry said of one another, and is poisoned for it." (쌍: 'It is a problem at which I have worked for the last seventeen years' ↔ -2 'So for twenty years altogether—counting nine years in England—I have been going on'; 'Tobermory can speak our language with perfect correctness.' ↔ -2 'I taught him the rudiments of English; gave him ideas of counting; even made the thing read the alphabet.'; 'broke loose and killed an Englishman who had apparently been teasing it' ↔ -2 'His head had been battered in by the fetters of the puma.'). 대체 테스트: Moreau → Frankenstein(피조물이 스스로 말을 배우고 Victor 아닌 가족을 죽임)·Rue Morgue(흉내 내는 원숭이)·Gulliver(태어날 때부터 말하는 말)·Invisible Man·Jekyll·Birth-Mark이면 거짓 → 통과. ② note Tobermory answers The Man That Corrupted Hadleyburg(lead 확인) / r:tobermory-hadleyburg-1 — claim "Both tales gather a respectable company to hear its members' private words made public one after another by an outsider who has watched them. Hadleyburg's men are exposed by lies they wrote for gold, and the town owns its fall and renames itself; Saki's guests are betrayed by a cat's ears, poison the witness, and learn nothing." (쌍: 'From a slight observation of your ways since you've been in this house…your own little affairs.' ↔ 'So I disguised myself and came back and studied you.'; 'Judging by what you said to Mrs. Cornett on the croquet-lawn yesterday, you were out for food.' ↔ 'name after name, and everybody had an increasingly and gloriously good time except the wretched Nineteen.'; 'Lady Blemley had sufficiently recovered her spirits to write an extremely nasty letter to the Rectory about the loss of her valuable pet.' ↔ 'It is an honest town once more, and the man will have to rise early that catches it napping again.'). 대체 테스트: Hadleyburg → Young Goodman Brown(악마가 죄인을 부르나 말을 옮기지 않음)·Scarlet Letter(한 사람의 자기 고백)·Scandal in Bohemia(왕 한 사람, 사진 한 장)·Candide·Bank-Note이면 거짓 → 통과. 실패한 lead 없음. 보류: Gulliver's Travels — Houyhnhnm 주인도 짐승 쪽에서 사람을 판단하나 말은 만들어진 게 아니고 엿들은 말이 아니라 Gulliver의 이야기를 판단함, 쌍 하나뿐 → work leads. heading:dethronement-of-man에 index 안 함: 범위는 사람을 짐승처럼 부리는 존재인데 Tobermory는 하루 만에 죽음 → work leads.
- [x] G11-07 2026-10-09 — The Lady with the Dog 입고(모두 proposed), `catalog.py check --stage G11-07` 오류 0·경고 0, `test_catalog.py --stage G11-07` 통과. person:anton-chekhov는 G11-05에서 이미 있음(작가 문서에 이 작품 항목도 이미 있음). work:lady-with-the-dog(story, ru; title·author ev = PG 13415 'Title: The Lady with the Dog and Other Stories'·'Author: Anton Pavlovich Chekhov'; first_pub 1899는 원문 근거 없음 → leads), text:lady-with-the-dog-garnett(Constance Garnett 역, The Tales of Chekhov vol. 3, PG 13415; 번역 연도는 PG에 없음 → leads). 새 heading:married-elsewhere('Love across a marriage'). index enacts: lady-with-the-dog → married-elsewhere('they could not understand why he had a wife and she a husband'), sorrows-of-young-werther → married-elsewhere('And what do they mean by saying Albert is your husband?'), scarlet-letter → married-elsewhere('Let, therefore, thy husband be to the world as one already dead'). context: 없음(Garnett 판에 서문·주 없음). 승인용 note·reading: ① note The Lady with the Dog answers The Necklace / r:lady-with-the-dog-necklace-1 (case moderate) — claim "A young wife married to a minor official she cannot look up to wants another life, finds it briefly among admirers away from home, then keeps a secret for years. Mathilde's one night costs ten years of drudgery repaying a borrowed jewel; Anna's holiday brings a lover who follows her home, and her secret has no end." (쌍: 'She was not sure whether her husband had a post in a Crown Department or under the Provincial Council' ↔ 'so she let herself be married to a little clerk of the Ministry of Public Instruction'; 'in his buttonhole there was some badge of distinction like the number on a waiter' ↔ 'Her husband had been sleeping since midnight in a little deserted anteroom'; 'they had still a long, long road before them…' ↔ 'This life lasted ten years.'). 대체 테스트: Necklace → Scarlet Letter(남편이 학자, 수치가 공개됨)·Werther(Charlotte는 Albert를 존중)·Yellow Wallpaper(존경받는 의사 남편, 바깥 나들이 없음)·Bank-Note·Overcoat(아내 없음)면 거짓 → 통과. ② note The Lady with the Dog answers The Scarlet Letter / r:lady-with-the-dog-scarlet-letter-1 (case moderate) — claim "A respected man loves a married woman in secret and keeps one face for the world. Hawthorne's minister cannot bear the split, Hester plans their flight, and he dies confessing; Chekhov's Gurov finds the hidden life the true one, and he and Anna plan and plan, the way out always about to appear." (쌍: 'He had two lives: one, open, seen and known by all who cared to know…' ↔ 'No man, for any considerable period, can wear one face to himself and another to the multitude'; 'the solution would be found, and then a new and splendid life would begin' ↔ 'Begin all anew!'; 'like "the woman who was a sinner" in an old-fashioned picture' ↔ 'an object to remind him of the image of Divine Maternity'). 대체 테스트: Scarlet Letter → Jekyll(이중생활은 있으나 연인·함께 짜는 탈출 계획 없음)·Minister's Black Veil·Young Goodman Brown(숨은 죄, 연인 없음)·Werther(이중생활·도피 계획 없음)면 거짓 → 통과. ③ note The Lady with the Dog answers The Sorrows of Young Werther / r:lady-with-the-dog-sorrows-of-young-werther-1 (case moderate; task leads에 없던 후보로, 새 heading에서 찾음) — claim "A lover cannot see why a marriage should stand between him and the woman fate meant for him. Werther loves alone, says farewell for ever and dies to claim Charlotte in the next world; Chekhov's lovers are both married, part for ever at a station, and meet again in this one, in secret, with no end." (쌍: 'they could not understand why he had a wife and she a husband' ↔ 'And what do they mean by saying Albert is your husband? He may be so for this world'; 'fate itself had meant them for one another' ↔ 'if thou hadst destined this happiness for me'; 'We are parting forever—it must be so, for we ought never to have met.' ↔ 'we shall recognise each other under every possible change!'). 대체 테스트: Werther → Scarlet Letter(연인들이 Hester의 혼인 자체를 따지지 않음)·Necklace(연인 없음)·Notes from Underground(Liza는 기혼 아님)·Dracula면 거짓 → 통과. contact: 우리 원문에 Chekhov가 Maupassant·Hawthorne·Goethe를 읽은 기록 없음 → 셋 다 none(leads). 실패·보류한 후보: lead The Bet(같은 작가) — Gurov는 은행원이고 모스크바 생활을 'a madhouse or a prison', 연인을 'different cages'의 철새라 하지만 The Bet의 감옥은 돈 때문에 스스로 고른 실제 감금이고 책 이야기; 남는 공통점('일상이 감옥')은 Notes from Underground·Yellow Wallpaper로 바꿔도 참 → 대체 테스트 실패, note 안 함. lead Vanka(같은 작가) — 공통점은 보낼 수 없는 쪽지뿐(Gurov는 'might fall into her husband's hands'라 쓰지도 않음, Vanka는 부침); '수신자에게 닿지 않는 편지'는 Bartleby의 dead letters로 감 → 거부. Jekyll and Hyde(두 개의 삶) — ②의 첫 쌍과 겹치고 연인·계획이 없어 따로 note 안 함. heading:double·hidden-sin — Gurov의 숨은 삶이 참된 쪽이라 분신(부정하는 것을 대신 행하는 둘째 자아)·숨은 죄(경건한 얼굴 뒤의 죄를 알게 돼 고립됨) 범위와 맞지 않음 → 색인 안 함. cites: 본문의 'The Geisha'·옛 그림 'the woman who was a sinner'는 라이브러리 작품 아님 → gloss 없음.

- [x] G12-02 2026-10-09 — Paul's Case 입고(모두 proposed), `catalog.py check --stage G12-02` 오류 0·경고 0, `test_catalog.py --stage G12-02` 실패 0. 새 person:willa-cather(LC n80015717 'Cather, Willa, 1873-1947', 작가 문서에 person 줄 추가). work:pauls-case(story; title·author ev = PG 13555 'PAUL'S CASE'·'Author: Willa Cather'; first_pub 1905 ev = Cather 노트 'published in 1905'; McClure's 1905년 5월 초출은 T0 → leads), text:pauls-case-1920(PG 13555 Youth and the Bright Medusa 1920 재수록본; 1905 Troll Garden과의 대조는 leads). 새 heading:life-staked-on-beauty('The life staked on beauty'). index enacts: pauls-case → life-staked-on-beauty('the only thing that could be called living at all'), dorian-gray → life-staked-on-beauty('the search for beauty being the real secret of life'). context: work:cather-youth-medusa-note(Cather가 Youth and the Bright Medusa 차례 뒤에 붙인 두 문단 노트, PG 13555) → pauls-case, role preface, content/contexts/cather-youth-medusa-note.txt. 승인용 note·reading: ① note Paul's Case answers The Picture of Dorian Gray / r:pauls-case-dorian-gray-1 (case moderate; lead 확인, 본문이 Wilde를 부르지 않아 cites 아님) — claim "Both tales follow a youth who finds the common world ugly, finds romance at a shabby theatre and lives for beautiful things until he dies by his own hand. Wilde's narrator says a book poisoned Dorian; Cather's says Paul scarcely read, and no portrait bears his sins: he wears his spoils for eight days and jumps." (쌍: 'his imagination had been perverted by garish fiction; but the truth was, he scarcely ever read at all.' ↔ 'Dorian Gray had been poisoned by a book.'; 'the stage entrance of that theatre was for Paul the actual portal of Romance' ↔ 'I passed by an absurd little theatre, with great flaring gas-jets and gaudy play-bills'; 'As he fell, the folly of his haste occurred to him with merciless clearness' ↔ 'It would kill the past, and when that was dead, he would be free.'). 대체 테스트: Dorian → Werther(책·자살은 있으나 극장·탓하는 책 없음)·Notes from Underground·The Bet(책은 있으나 극장·자살 없음)·Necklace·Jekyll·Lady with the Dog·Tobermory면 거짓 → 통과. ② note Paul's Case answers The Necklace / r:pauls-case-necklace-1 (case moderate; leads에 없던 후보) — claim "Both tales give someone from a thrifty home, who feels born for luxury, one bright spell among the rich on money not their own. Mathilde pays for a borrowed paste necklace with ten years of scrubbing and lives on; Paul's father repays the theft at once, and Paul jumps rather than go home." (쌍: 'these were his own people, he told himself' ↔ 'feeling herself born to enjoy all delicacies and all luxuries.'; 'He reflected upon the mysterious dishes that were brought into the dining-room…' ↔ 'she thought of delicious dishes served on marvellous plates'; 'the boy's father had refunded the full amount of his theft' ↔ 'At the end of ten years they had paid everything, everything'). 대체 테스트: Necklace → Bank-Note(빌린 돈을 쓰지도 갚지도 않음)·Overcoat(사치를 원하지 않음)·Dorian(처음부터 부자)·Hadleyburg·Story of an Hour·Lady with the Dog·Tobermory면 거짓 → 통과. contact: 우리 원문에 Cather가 Wilde·Maupassant를 읽은 기록 없음 → 둘 다 none(Cather의 1890년대 신문 칼럼은 T0 → leads). 실패·보류한 후보: Yellow Wallpaper — Paul의 위층 방 'horrible yellow wallpaper'가 겹치나 둘째·셋째 대응 구절이 없는 모티프 하나뿐 → note 안 냄(work leads). heading:borrowed-riches에 index 안 함: Paul의 돈은 빌린 게 아니라 훔친 것이고 돌려줄 기한이 없음. cites: 본문의 Arabian Nights 어부·Faust·Martha·Rigoletto·Wordsworth 'a sleep and a forgetting'은 이 단계 그래프의 작품이 아님 → gloss 없음. 참고: begin 뒤 Lady with the Dog·Tobermory가 병합돼 두 대체 테스트에 넣음(둘 다 거짓).

(2) araby answers northanger-abbey + r:araby-northanger-abbey-1(case moderate; 'The syllables of the word Araby' ↔ 'Northanger Abbey! these were thrilling words', 'porcelain vases and flowered tea-sets' ↔ 'elegance of modern taste', 'driven and derided by vanity' ↔ 'The visions of romance were over') — claim: 본 적 없는 곳의 이름에 홀린 소녀와 소년이 그곳에 가서 낭만 대신 유행하는 세간을 보고 자기 공상을 어리석음으로 판정하지만, Catherine은 Henry의 꾸짖음으로 깨어나 하루 만에 자신을 용서하고, Araby의 소년은 아무 말도 듣지 못한 채 홀로 고뇌와 분노 속에 깬다. 대체 테스트: 두 reading 모두 begin 목록의 비-paratext 작품 전부 + substitute peers(dorian-gray, northanger-abbey / 고딕 저택 6편·yellow-wallpaper)로 시험해 claim이 거짓 → 통과. leads 판정: Paul's Case(소년이 꿈꾸던 장소에서 환멸) — 원문 확인, 단 Paul은 Waldorf에서 환멸을 보지 않으므로 환멸 자체가 아니라 '들어가지 못함/돈'과 '들어가서 꺼짐'의 대비로 answers 성립. 실패·보류 후보: 본문이 부르는 Scott의 The Abbot·Memoirs of Vidocq·'The Arab's Farewell to his Steed'는 라이브러리 작품이 아니라 gloss·cites 없음. context: content/contexts에 Joyce·Dubliners 서문·편지·서평 없음(1906 Grant Richards 편지는 T0) → 없음.

II; PG의 'Notes—Maelstrom' 각주가 우리 사본엔 없음 → copy-text leads). 새 heading:out-of-the-whirlpool('Out of the whirlpool'). index enacts: descent-into-the-maelstrom('I resolved to lash myself securely to the water cask upon which I now held'), moby-dick('Buoyed up by that coffin, for almost one whole day and night…'), twenty-thousand-leagues('…how Ned Land, Conseil, and myself ever came out of the gulf, I cannot tell.'). context: 없음(content/contexts에 이 작품의 서문·편지·서평 없음 → leads). 승인용 note·reading: ① note Moby-Dick rewrites (inversion) A Descent into the Maelström / r:moby-dick-descent-into-the-maelstrom-1 (case moderate) — claim "Both end with one man alive from a boat drawn round into a whirl and lifted out on a floating cask or coffin. Poe's fisherman reasons from what the whirl spares, lashes himself to the cask and leaves his brother; Ishmael, dropped astern by chance, does nothing, and the coffin bursts up to him." (쌍: 'Round and round, then, and ever contracting towards the button-like black bubble…like another Ixion I did revolve.' ↔ 'Round and round we swept—not with any uniform movement—but in dizzying swings and jerks'; 'the coffin life-buoy shot lengthwise from the sea, fell over, and floated by my side' ↔ 'I resolved to lash myself securely to the water cask…'; 'It so chanced…I was he whom the Fates ordained to take the place of Ahab’s bowsman' ↔ 'with a bitter struggle, I resigned him to his fate'). contact none(본문이 Poe를 부르지 않음, Melville의 독서 기록 없음 → leads). ② note Twenty Thousand Leagues rewrites (inversion) A Descent into the Maelström / r:twenty-thousand-leagues-descent-into-the-maelstrom-1 (case moderate) — claim "Verne ends his voyage in Poe's whirl off Lofoden: whales and a bear swallowed, a small boat spinning till the teller is giddy, rescue by Lofoden fishermen, a story that may not be believed. But Poe's fisherman stays awake and tells exactly how he got out; Aronnax is knocked senseless and cannot tell." (쌍: 'There, not only vessels, but whales are sacrificed, as well as white bears from the northern regions.' ↔ 'A bear once, attempting to swim from Lofoden to Moskoe, was caught by the stream and borne down'; 'My head struck on a piece of iron, and with the violent shock I lost all consciousness.' ↔ 'I took courage, and looked once again upon the scene.'; 'Shall I be believed? I do not know. And it matters little, after all.' ↔ 'I told them my story—they did not believe it.'). 대체 테스트(begin 목록의 소설·단편 52편, 둘 다 통과): Maelström 자리에 Twenty Thousand Leagues(아론낙스는 의식을 잃고 뜰 것에 매달리지 않음)·Journey to the Centre(뗏목이 소용돌이에서 돌지만 잃는 사람·떠오르게 하는 통이 없고 Stromboli로 나옴)·Usher(화자는 걸어서 달아남)·Moby-Dick(로포텐·곰·어부 없음, 이슈메일은 Rachel 호가 건짐)·Rip(믿지 않는 이야기지만 바다 없음)·Candide·Gulliver·Frankenstein(난파·얼음은 있으나 소용돌이·통 없음)을 넣으면 claim이 거짓. leads 판정: TTL cites 실패 — 'The maelstrom!'은 장소 이름이지 Poe의 작품이 아니고, 본문의 'Edgar Poe'(7장)는 헤엄 잘 치는 사람으로 부를 뿐 이 작품을 가리키지 않아 refers note가 안 됨 → close-reading rewrites로 냄(고래·흰곰·나무는 Poe가 인용한 Jonas Ramus에도 있어 Ramus·백과사전 경유 가능성은 note leads에 적음; 로포텐 어부의 구조와 '믿을까'는 Poe 쪽). Moby-Dick 후보는 확인 → rewrites로 냄.

Glaspell's wife has already acted and is gone; her song and bird are dead, and her neighbors name the crime and keep the reason from the men." (쌍: 'That was a crime! … Who's going to punish that?' ↔ 'A kind intention or a cruel intention made the act seem no less a crime'; 'a thing that sang. She used to sing. He killed that too.' ↔ 'The notes of a distant song … countless sparrows were twittering in the eaves.'; 'it's all perfectly clear, except the reason for doing it.' ↔ 'they said she had died of heart disease — of the joy that kills.'). ③ note Jury of Her Peers answers The Purloined Letter / r:jury-of-her-peers-purloined-letter-1 (case moderate) — claim "Poe's Prefect laughs at the hint that the case is too plain; Dupin reads a soiled letter against its owner's neat habits and pockets it for the police. Glaspell's men laugh at quilting talk; two wives read one crooked block against Minnie's even sewing and pocket the dead bird to keep it from the law." (쌍: 'There was a laugh for the ways of women' ↔ '"What nonsense you do talk!" replied the Prefect, laughing heartily.'; 'All the rest of them have been so nice and even—but—this one.' ↔ 'the soiled and torn condition of the paper, so inconsistent with the true methodical habits of D——'; 'Martha Hale snatched the box … in the pocket of her big coat' ↔ 'took the letter, put it in my pocket, and replaced it by a fac-simile'). 대체 테스트(begin 뷰의 story·novel 55편): ① Story of an Hour(웃음·목·밧줄 없음)·Birth-Mark·Usher·Purloined Letter면 거짓 → 통과; ② Yellow Wallpaper(죄라 부르지 않음, 노래·새·사후 판정 없음)·Rip·Birth-Mark면 거짓 → 통과; ③ Rue Morgue(주머니에 넣지 않음)·Styles(장식 두 번 정돈은 읽지만 아무도 웃지 않고 증거는 결국 법으로)·Scandal in Bohemia(사진을 못 얻음)·Blue Cross(꾸러미를 바꾸나 습관 어긋남 읽기 없음, 십자가를 돌려줌)면 거짓 → 통과. contact: 우리 원문에 Glaspell이 Gilman·Chopin·Poe를 읽은 기록 없음 → none(leads). 실패·보류한 후보: lead Yellow Wallpaper·Story of an Hour(갇힌 아내, 여자들만 읽는 단서)는 ①②로 따로 확인 — '부엌의 단서를 여자들만 읽음'만으로는 두 작품 어느 쪽으로 바꿔도 참이라 각 note를 그 작품에만 있는 구절(벽지의 부러진 목·밧줄 / crime·노래와 새·의사의 판정)로 좁힘. lead A3 탐정(남자 수사관이 단서를 놓침): '경찰이 놓친 단서를 아마추어가 읽음'은 heading:detection 수준이라 Rue Morgue·Styles·Scandal·Blue Cross·Study in Scarlet 어디로 바꿔도 참 → 이 일반형은 index(detection)로만 두고 note는 세 쌍이 모두 맞는 Purloined Letter 하나(③). (쌍: 'I’ll remember it again after the party’s over, she decided.' ↔ 'I will not let any gloomy moralising intrude upon us here tonight.'; 'There lay a young man, fast asleep—…far, far away from them both.' ↔ 'he imagined he saw the form of a young man standing under a dripping tree.'; 'But what life was she couldn’t explain.' ↔ 'He was conscious of, but could not apprehend, their wayward and flickering existence.'). ② note The Garden Party answers The Necklace / r:garden-party-necklace-1 (case moderate, contact none; leads 밖 후보, 원문으로 확인) — claim "Each girl sees herself made beautiful in a mirror, is the beauty of the party, and afterwards wants to hide her clothes from women of another class. (쌍: 'this charming girl in the mirror' ↔ 'lost in ecstasy at her reflection in the mirror.'; 'Laura, you look quite Spanish. Melville's lawyer has only a rumour and is horrified; Anderson tells Wing's ruin as fact and shows him eating crumbs like a priest at prayer." (쌍: 'The story of Wing Biddlebaum’s hands is worth a book in itself. … It is a job for a poet.' ↔ 'I believe that no materials exist for a full and satisfactory biography of this man. It is an irreparable loss to literature.'; 'In his youth Wing Biddlebaum had been a school teacher in a town in Pennsylvania.' ↔ 'The report was this: that Bartleby had been a subordinate clerk in the Dead Letter Office at Washington…'; 'A few stray white bread crumbs lay on the cleanly washed floor …' ↔ 'in a newspaper a few crumbs of ginger-nuts and a morsel of cheese'). ② note Winesburg, Ohio answers Araby / r:winesburg-ohio-araby-1 (case moderate; 'Sophistication') — claim "A boy at a fair emptied of its crowd hears its silence and sees himself as a small thing driven along, his feeling for a girl unspoken. Araby's boy stands there without her and ends in anguish and anger; George sits silent beside Helen in the dark grand-stand, and the silence gives both what they need." (쌍: '…now it is night and the life has all gone away. The silence is almost terrifying.' ↔ 'I recognised a silence like that which pervades a church after a service.'; 'With a little gasp he sees himself as merely a leaf blown by the wind through the streets of his village.' ↔ 'Gazing up into the darkness I saw myself as a creature driven and derided by vanity…'; 'In the darkness in the grand-stand Helen White and George Willard remained silent.' ↔ '…how I could tell her of my confused adoration.'). ③ note Winesburg, Ohio answers The Dead / r:winesburg-ohio-the-dead-1 (case speculative; 'Sophistication') — claim "After a night of music and dancing, a man in the dark beside a woman thinks of all who lived before him, feels his self dwindle, and weeps. Gabriel, beside a wife who loved a dead boy, fades into the snow; George weeps for love of his living town, and Helen makes his smallness bearable." (쌍: 'the countless figures of men who before his time have come out of nothingness into the world, lived their lives and again disappeared into nothingness' ↔ 'His soul had approached that region where dwell the vast hosts of the dead.'; '…felt very keenly his own insignificance in the scheme of existence.' ↔ 'His own identity was fading out into a grey impalpable world…'; '…one loves life so intensely that tears come into the eyes.' ↔ 'Generous tears filled Gabriel’s eyes.'). 대체 테스트: 세 reading 모두 begin 목록의 비-paratext 작품 59편 전부 + substitute peers(araby·overcoat·vanka / northanger-abbey / araby·lady-with-the-dog)로 시험해 claim이 거짓 → 통과(Overcoat는 Akaky의 출생·이름을 처음에 말하고 잃은 직책을 나중에 밝히지 않음; Northanger는 소녀·저택이지 빈 장터가 아님; Lady with the Dog의 Oreanda 장면엔 춤도 눈물도 없음; Werther의 춤·폭풍·눈물은 Klopstock 이름에서 오지 자아가 죽은 이들 앞에서 줄어드는 게 아님). contact: Anderson이 Bartleby·Dubliners를 읽은 기록이 우리 자료에 없음(Dubliners 1916 미국판과 Winesburg가 같은 Huebsch에서 나온 것은 T0) → 셋 다 none(leads). 실패·보류 후보: Dead·Araby shares-form(이어진 단편집 형식) — 그 형식은 책 Dubliners의 것이고 Dubliners는 그래프에 work로 없으며 Araby·The Dead는 단편 하나씩이라 작품 단위로 물려받을 형식이 없음 → shares-form 대신 'Sophistication' 구절로 answers 두 개를 냄. cites: 본문이 부르는 것은 성경(Jesse·David)뿐, 라이브러리 작품 아님 → gloss 없음. context: content/contexts에 Winesburg 서문·편지·서평 없음(PG 416의 Howe 서문은 PD 아님; 1919 서평·A Story Teller's Story는 T0) → 없음.

(쌍: 'the captain would carry me gratis to Lisbon' ↔ 'I will carry you thither in charity'; 'abhorring to cover myself with any thing that had been on the back of a Yahoo' ↔ 'with a very good suit of clothes of his own, which had been worn but very little'; 'Don Pedro accompanied me to the ship, and lent me twenty pounds' ↔ 'he pulls out an old pouch, and gives me one hundred and sixty Portugal moidores in gold'). ② note Robinson Crusoe answers Sindbad / r:robinson-crusoe-sindbad-1 (case moderate, contact none) — claim "Both castaways stand alone on a wreck's shore among riches that cannot feed them, build a raft from the wreckage and blame their love of roving. Sindbad loads his raft with rubies and trusts his lucky star; Crusoe loads bread and cheese, keeps the coin only on second thoughts, and calls his roving an original sin." (쌍: 'what art thou good for? Thou art not worth to me' ↔ 'but all these things only added to the desolation of the scene'; 'the first of these I filled with provisions—viz. bread, rice, three Dutch cheeses' ↔ 'I then made up many packages of rubies, emeralds, … and bound them upon my raft'; 'my original sin' ↔ 'I regretted bitterly the roving disposition which was always bringing me into such straits'). ③ note Connecticut Yankee refers Robinson Crusoe (계산, gloss:connecticut-yankee-crusoe, mode alludes, contact documented) — gloss 'I saw that I was just another Robinson Crusoe cast away on an uninhabited island' → Crusoe 'every man may be, in time, master of every mechanic art'. 대체 테스트(begin 뷰의 story·novel 56편, 병합 뒤 Winesburg·Garden Party 포함): ① Sindbad(포르투갈·리스본·옷 없음)·Candide(리스본엔 난파로, 선장 아닌 Jacques)·Moreau(포르투갈 배·옷·돈 없음)면 거짓 → 통과; ② Gulliver(Yahoo 가죽 카누, 보물 없음)·Journey to the Centre(뗏목뿐)·Twenty Thousand Leagues(Vigo 금, 뗏목·조난자 없음)·Moby-Dick·Moreau면 거짓 → 통과. leads 결과: Gulliver rewrites Crusoe 통과(①, 단 후반 Houyhnhnm 귀환부에서만 구절 단위로 성립 — Lilliput 주머니 목록 ↔ 난파선 물품 목록은 목록일 뿐 장면 변형이 아니라 안 씀; Gulliver의 'Captain William Robinson'은 이름뿐이라 gloss 아님 → contact none). Sindbad 통과(②). 내지 않은 후보: Crusoe의 맹세·'something fatal in that propension'(떠돎을 운명 탓) ↔ Sindbad 'my fate to rove'는 대체 테스트에서 Gulliver('evil destiny', 이미 Gulliver–Sindbad reading이 씀)로 바꿔도 참 → 버림. 뒤 단계 작품(Treasure Island·Moonstone 등)이 Crusoe를 부르는 구절은 그 단계 몫.

(c) araby·winesburg-ohio > words-that-fail index는 이 단계 reading의 peers·contact 판단을 바꾸지 않고, 이 단계가 쓴 항목 중 없어진 것도 없음. `catalog.py check --stage G12-07` 오류 0·경고 0.

(쌍: 'Someone would appear there at the turn of a path and would stand before me and smile and approve.' ↔ 'No Gytrash was this,—only a traveller taking the short cut to Millcote.' -2; 'an insane, an unmentionable relative kept in unsuspected confinement' ↔ 'Bertha Mason is mad; and she came of a mad family …' -4; 'she couldn’t tell her story without its coming out' ↔ 'Portrait of a Governess, disconnected, poor, and plain.' -2). ② note Jane Eyre answers Northanger Abbey(lead 확인) / r:jane-eyre-northanger-abbey-1 — claim "Both heroines sense a secret behind an old house's shut doors and laugh off gothic fears with a household trifle. Catherine's prisoner wife is her invention, and a washing-bill and Henry's 'we are English' cure her; Jane mocks her alarm with Grace Poole's pot of porter and is wrong: the wife is there." (쌍: 'like a corridor in some Bluebeard’s castle' -2 ↔ 'receiving from the pitiless hands of her husband a nightly supply of coarse food' -2; 'oh, romantic reader, forgive me for telling the plain truth!' -2 ↔ 'An inventory of linen, in coarse and modern characters, seemed all that was before her!' -2; '“That is my wife,” said he.' -4 ↔ 'Remember that we are English, that we are Christians.' -2). ③ note The Yellow Wallpaper answers Jane Eyre(leads 밖 후보, 원문으로 확인; 1892가 뒤라 from YW) / r:yellow-wallpaper-jane-eyre-1 — claim "Both put a wife, shut in by her husband, in a room at the top of the house, going on all fours. Brontë shows Bertha from outside, through the governess who sees her face under her own bridal veil; Gilman tells it from inside the barred room, where the wife becomes the creeping woman." (쌍: 'the windows are barred for little children, and there are rings and things in the walls.' ↔ 'In a room without a window, there burnt a fire guarded by a high and strong fender, and a lamp suspended from the ceiling by a chain.' -4; 'It is the same woman, I know, for she is always creeping' ↔ 'it grovelled, seemingly, on all fours; it snatched and growled like some strange wild animal' -4; 'I’ve got out at last,” said I, “in spite of you and Jane!' ↔ 'At that moment I saw the reflection of the visage and features quite distinctly in the dark oblong glass.' -4; YW 끝의 'Jane'은 인유로 보지 않음). 내지 않은 후보: Pride and Prejudice(heading:the-offer-refused — St John의 청혼은 부양하는 혼처가 아니고 그때 Jane은 유산을 받았음, Rochester의 제안은 정부(情婦) 자리라 서가 범위 밖, 답하는 구절 없음); House of the Seven Gables·Usher(heading:haunted-house — Thornfield가 숨긴 건 살아 있는 아내지 집이 대물림하는 가문의 과거가 아님). gloss 못 단 것: 'Arabian tales'·'the Arabian Nights'(모음집, work:sindbad 이름 없음), Rasselas(G35 입고 뒤 refers 후보), Bewick·Pamela(서고 밖), 서문의 Vanity Fair(G37 뒤 후보). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

Austen's narrator defends novels, and Catherine, shamed out of her fancies, owns them her own; Flaubert leaves the charge to Emma's mother-in-law, and Emma, let down, decides that life itself is the lie." (쌍: 'Emma, at fifteen years of age, made her hands dirty with books from old lending libraries' ↔ 'from fifteen to seventeen she was in training for a heroine'; 'So it was decided to stop Emma reading novels.' ↔ 'Yes, novels; for I will not adopt that ungenerous and impolitic custom…'; 'nothing was worth the trouble of seeking it; everything was a lie' ↔ 'The visions of romance were over.'). ② note The Lady with the Dog answers Madame Bovary / r:lady-with-the-dog-madame-bovary-1 (case moderate, contact none; lead 확인, Chekhov 1899가 뒤라 from Lady with the Dog) — claim "Both follow a young wife bored by her husband into a lover's room. Emma's first fall is a triumph, out of her books, with a practised lover who finds her like all the rest, and the adultery sinks into marriage's platitudes; Anna's is a shame, and her practised lover, grey-haired, first loves her as a wife." (쌍: 'I am a bad, low woman; I despise myself…' ↔ 'She tasted it without remorse, without anxiety, without trouble.'; 'but he had never once loved; it was anything you like, but not love.' ↔ 'Emma was like all his mistresses'; '…loved each other like people very close and akin, like husband and wife' ↔ 'Emma found again in adultery all the platitudes of marriage.'). ③ note The Necklace answers Madame Bovary / r:necklace-madame-bovary-1 (case moderate, contact none; leads 밖 후보, 원문으로 확인; Neveux 연구(PG 3090)의 Maupassant가 7년간 Flaubert에게 원고를 냈다는 구절은 note leads에만) — claim "Both wives, married below their dreams, sit across from a husband pleased with his dinner, are lifted by one grand ball, and live on its memory. Mathilde is the ball's beauty and pays in ten years of toil; Emma dances once, brings home a stranger's cigar-case, and makes the ball a dream of Paris." (쌍: 'opposite her husband, who uncovered the soup tureen…' ↔ 'Even at table she had her book by her, and turned over the pages while Charles ate and talked to her.'; 'She danced with rapture, with passion, intoxicated by pleasure' ↔ 'Her journey to Vaubyessard had made a hole in her life'; '…of that ball where she had been so beautiful and so admired.' ↔ 'The memory of this ball, then, became an occupation for Emma.'). 대체 테스트(begin 목록의 story·novel: Northanger·Pride and Prejudice·Lady with the Dog·Necklace·Story of an Hour·Scarlet Letter·Werther·Turn of the Screw·Yellow Wallpaper·Garden Party·The Dead·Jury of Her Peers·Notes from Underground·Paul's Case·Araby·Dorian Gray, begin 재실행 뒤 Jane Eyre·Winesburg, Ohio·Robinson Crusoe 추가): ① Turn of the Screw(Udolpho를 알지만 열다섯 소녀도, 독서 심판도 없음)·Notes from Underground(책으로 삶을 배운 남자, 소설 심판 없음)·Jane Eyre(열 살에 Bewick·Gulliver)면 거짓 → 통과; ② Scarlet Letter(여관 방·노련한 유혹자 없음)·Jane Eyre(정부 되기를 거절)·Werther면 거짓 → 통과; ③ Northanger·P&P(무도회는 있으나 미혼, 사치 꿈 없음)·Garden Party(남편 없음)·Paul's Case(결혼 없음)·Jane Eyre(손님 파티를 보는 가정교사)면 거짓 → 통과. 내지 않은 후보: The Story of an Hour(결혼을 속박으로 느끼는 것뿐, Louise의 해방은 전해 들은 죽음으로 한 시간 — 불륜·독서·빚에 답하는 구절 없음); The Scarlet Letter(소도시 불륜과 목사의 눈 — 맞는 구절(수치를 나누지 않는 연인)이 Lady with the Dog에도 맞아 대체 테스트 실패); The Voyages of Sindbad(Homais 기사의 'Thousand and One Nights'는 모음집 전체, Sindbad를 가리키지 않아 gloss 없음). cites: Paul and Virginia·Génie du Christianisme·Lamartine·Walter Scott·Sue·Balzac·George Sand·Voltaire·Rousseau·Béranger는 라이브러리 작품 아님 → gloss 없음. site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

His maroon's piety comes with rum and buried gold, the parrot cries for money, and the men left behind beg not to be left to die." (쌍: 'But it were Providence that put me here. … I’m back on piety.' ↔ 'I had been well instructed by father and mother' -2; 'start upright in bed with the sharp voice of Captain Flint still ringing in my ears' ↔ 'I was awaked out of my sleep by a voice calling me by my name several times' -2; 'to take them home for the gibbet would have been a cruel sort of kindness' ↔ 'if the captain had carried them away they would certainly be hanged.' -4). ② note Treasure Island answers Moby-Dick(leads 밖 후보, 원문으로 확인) / r:treasure-island-moby-dick-1 — claim "Both voyages hang on a one-legged seaman an owner vouches for, who braces his leg in the deck and holds out gold to the crew. Ahab lost his leg to the whale he hunts, stands rigid, and pays for revenge; Silver lies about his leg, sways with every roll, and wants the money." (쌍: 'has lost a leg; but that I regarded as a recommendation, since he lost it in his country’s service, under the immortal Hawke.' ↔ 'ever since he lost his leg last voyage by that accursed whale, he’s been a kind of moody' -1; 'wedge the foot of the crutch against a bulkhead … yielding to every movement of the ship' ↔ 'His bone leg steadied in that hole; one arm elevated, and holding by a shroud; Captain Ahab stood erect' -2; 'it’s hundreds of pounds instead of hundreds of farthings in their pockets' ↔ 'he shall have this gold ounce, my boys!' -2). 대체 테스트: ①은 Sindbad(앵무새·반란 없음)·Gulliver(제 선원이 반란해 그를 내려놓지만 섬에 남기는 사람 없음)·Moreau(신앙·반란자 유기 없음)면 거짓, ②는 그래프의 다른 작품에 외다리 선원이 없음(Nemo·Maelström 어부·Crusoe의 선장 모두 해당 없음) → 둘 다 통과. leads 결과: Crusoe — 본문이 Crusoe·Defoe를 부르지 않아 cites(gloss·refers) 아님 → 구절 단위 rewrites로 냄. Stevenson 에세이의 'No doubt the parrot once belonged to Robinson Crusoe'·'A few reminiscences of Poe, Defoe, and Washington Irving'은 1차 paratext의 독서 기록이지만 read note는 encounter(B2)에서 계산되므로 contact none, note leads에 적음. Ben Gunn의 염소 가죽·염소·가죽 배는 공유 소품이라 쌍으로 쓰지 않음. Gold-Bug — 이 단계 그래프에 없음(G16) → 보물 지도·해골 판정은 그 단계 몫(에세이 'No doubt the skeleton is conveyed from Poe'·'stole Flint’s pointer', heading:treasure-map 공유 후보). 내지 않은 후보: Sindbad(부자로 귀향한 뒤 다시 바다로 ↔ Jim의 'Oxen and wain-ropes would not bring me back')는 Crusoe도 섬을 다시 찾아가 대체 테스트에서 참 → 버림; heading:castaway는 범위가 '지나가는 배가 신호를 보고 구조'인데 Ben Gunn은 신호 없이 Jim을 만나 enacts 아님. gloss 없음(본문이 부르는 Bible·Hawke·Kidd·England·Roberts는 서고 작품 아님; Irving Tales of a Traveller·Masterman Ready·Johnson's Buccaneers·Kingsley At Last는 서고 밖). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

(쌍: 'When it occurs to a man that nature does not regard him as important…' ↔ 'how foolish it was in me to think of so paltry a consideration as my own individual life, in view of so wonderful a manifestation of God’s power'; 'Viewed from a balcony, the whole thing would doubtlessly have been weirdly picturesque. Crusoe 확인 → note ①(Crane이 겪은 일이라 차용 아닌 같은 질문 → answers). 본문 인용 'A soldier of the Legion lay dying in Algiers'(Caroline Norton)·'babes in the wood'는 서고 작품이 아니라 gloss 없음. The squall! The squall! Shove off!’' ↔ 'The mind of the master of a vessel is rooted deep in the timbers of her…'). ② note Lord Jim answers Moby-Dick / r:lord-jim-moby-dick-1 — claim "Both young seamen leap from their post in a fright they cannot explain, are cursed as cowards in the boat, and are left facing the sea's immensity. (쌍: 'of the cause that tore him out of his immobility, he knew no more than the uprooted tree knows of the wind that laid it low' ↔ 'The involuntary consternation of the moment caused him to leap, paddle in hand, out of the boat'; 'Where did you get the pluck to jump—you coward?' ↔ 'the poor little negro was assailed by yells and execrations from the crew'; 'A silence of the sea, of the sky, merged into one indefinite immensity still as death around these saved, palpitating lives.' ↔ 'The intense concentration of self in the middle of such a heartless immensity, my God! who can tell it?'). 대체 테스트(begin 뷰의 novel·novella·story 65편, 둘 다 통과): ① Open Boat 자리에 Crusoe(보트가 곧 뒤집혀 선원이 바로 익사)·Gulliver(돌풍에 전복)·Sindbad·Moby-Dick(홀로 구조)·Maelström(제 어선, 잃은 배 없음)·Twenty Thousand Leagues(Nautilus는 가라앉지 않음)·Treasure Island(jolly-boat, 배는 떠 있음)를 넣으면 거짓; ② Moby-Dick 자리에 Open Boat(선장 명령으로 뛰어듦)·Maelström(계획대로 통에 묶고 뛰어듦)·Treasure Island(Jim Hawkins는 충동으로 보트에 숨고 욕먹지 않음)·Crusoe·Gulliver·Sindbad(겁에 질린 도약 없음)를 넣으면 거짓. leads 판정: Open Boat·Moby-Dick 둘 다 answers 확인 → note ①②. (쌍: 'First reason: Because Sir Henry Curtis and Captain John Good asked me.' ↔ 'Squire Trelawney, Dr. Livesey, and the rest of these gentlemen having asked me to write down…'; 'There he sat, the dead man, whose directions… had led us to this spot.' ↔ 'The body pointed straight in the direction of the island…'; 'but I do not think I would go through the last fifteen or sixteen months again for it' ↔ 'Oxen and wain-ropes would not bring me back again to that accursed island'). ② note The Lost World answers King Solomon's Mines(lead 확인; Doyle 1912가 뒤라 from Lost World) / r:lost-world-king-solomons-mines-1 — claim "Both parties follow a dead forerunner's marks into a walled-off country, win a native people's war with rifles, and bring home diamonds known by Kimberley's blue clay. (쌍: 'He had some presentiment that worthy footsteps would follow close behind him.' ↔ 'a sad memento of the fate that so often overtakes those who would penetrate into the unknown'; 'since by our strange powers we had aided them to destroy their hereditary foe' ↔ 'No other white man shall cross the mountains…'; 'That was the great De Beers Diamond Mine of Kimberley—what?…' ↔ 'Then it is clear that you have never seen the diamond diggings at Kimberley…'). 대체 테스트(begin 뷰의 novel·story·novella 66편, 작업 중 병합된 Lord Jim 포함 → 둘 다 passed): ①은 Lost World(분필 화살표·스케치북, Colver 해골은 길을 가리키지 않음, Lord John은 다시 가려 함)·Journey to the Centre(암호·새긴 이니셜, 시신 없음)·Robinson Crusoe(섬을 다시 찾음)·Sindbad(다시 항해)·Lord Jim이면 거짓; ②는 Treasure Island(원주민 전쟁·다이아몬드 없음)·Connecticut Yankee(총으로 이기지만 앞서 간 죽은 이·광산 없음)·Lord Jim(Patusan에서 Doramin의 전쟁을 총으로 이기지만 죽은 이의 표시·다이아몬드 없음)·Journey·Gulliver면 거짓. 떨어뜨린 후보 없음(leads 둘 다 원문 구절로 확인). (쌍: 'It was Hyde, after all, and Hyde alone, that was guilty.' ↔ '…the organ that he called his conscience declared itself content.'; 'I stood already committed to a profound duplicity of life.' ↔ 'The more things are wrong the more we must act as if all were right.'; '…that ugly idol in the glass, … a leap of welcome.' ↔ 'A horrible, ugly look came and went across his almost venerable countenance.'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Moreau(산 짐승을 사서 자름)·Dracula(Lucy의 무덤을 여는 건 없애려는 것, 해부 수업 없음)·Usher(지하실은 의사들로부터 지켜짐)·Jekyll(시체 없음)이면 거짓, Frankenstein 자리는 Moreau·Rappaccini·Birth-Mark(산 몸에 작업)이면 거짓; ②는 Dorian Gray(의사 아님, 초상이 죄를 짐)·Scarlet Letter(Dimmesdale의 양심은 쉬지 못함)·Moonstone(Ablewhite의 이중생활, 의사 아님)·Frankenstein(양심이 쉬지 못함)이면 거짓, Jekyll 자리는 Dorian·Invisible Man(투명해질 뿐 둘이 되지 않음)·Rappaccini이면 거짓. 실패한 후보 없음(leads 둘 다 원문 구절로 확인). 내지 않은 것: heading:double(Macfarlane의 표정·Fettes의 술 깸은 한 사람의 순간, 두 번째 자아 아님), heading:hidden-sin(Fettes는 남의 죄를 알아채는 게 아니라 함께 짓는다); Usher answers(가족 쪽에서 본 도굴 걱정 — lead 아님, leads에 적음); PG 622의 1881년 6월 'The Black Man and Other Tales' 목록 편지·7월 'scrolled' 편지는 dossier 후보로 leads. site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

P. Cooke 'Edgar A. Poe', Southern Literary Messenger 1848-01; 새 person:philip-pendleton-cooke LC no2009180542, authority 'Cooke, Philip Pendleton, 1816-1850' 일치; archive.org OCR에서 발췌 전사, 정정 23건 cooke-edgar-a-poe.emend.jsonl, 빠진 앞뒤는 […]) → tell-tale-heart('the visible vulture eye, and audible heart-beat in the “Tell-tale Heart”'), → fall-of-the-house-of-usher(Usher 마지막 문단 인용), → descent-into-the-maelstrom('the descending round of the vortex'), role 모두 criticism. 대체 테스트(이 단계 뷰의 소설·단편·중편 84편 → 모두 passed): ① note The Tell-Tale Heart rewrites The Fall of the House of Usher(mode transposition; lead '같은 작가' 확인 — 장면 자체를 옮겨 와 answers 대신 rewrites) / r:tell-tale-heart-fall-of-the-house-of-usher-1, case strong — claim "Poe gives the close of Usher to the murderer himself: a man of acute senses hears the heart of one he put under the floor and shrieks his secret. Usher's sister was buried alive and returns; the old man was killed and stays dead, so the beat can come only from the narrator." (쌍: 'And have I not told you that what you mistake for madness is but over-acuteness of the sense?' ↔ 'Said I not that my senses were acute?'; 'It is the beating of his hideous heart!' ↔ 'Do I not distinguish that heavy and horrible beating of her heart?'; '“Villains!” I shrieked, “dissemble no more! I admit the deed!' ↔ 'Madman! I tell you that she now stands without the door!'). ② note Notes from Underground answers The Tell-Tale Heart(lead 확인; contact none, Vremya 1861의 Poe 번역은 T0 → leads) / r:notes-from-underground-tell-tale-heart-1, case moderate — claim "Both narrators answer a 'you' who thinks them mad by blaming an excess of perception. Poe's over-acute hearing drives him to kill and then to shriek his deed to the police; Dostoevsky's over-acute consciousness leaves him unable to act at all, and he writes his confession for himself alone." (쌍: 'You think, gentlemen, perhaps that I am mad? Allow me to defend myself.' ↔ 'but why will you say that I am mad?'; '…the normal fundamental laws of over-acute consciousness, and with the inertia…' ↔ 'what you mistake for madness is but over-acuteness of the sense'; 'Such confessions as I intend to make are never printed nor given to other people to read.' ↔ 'I admit the deed!—tear up the planks!—here, here!'). ③ note The Diamond Lens answers The Tell-Tale Heart(task lead 아님 — 뷰 전체에서 'I am mad' 구절 검색으로 찾음; contact none) / r:diamond-lens-tell-tale-heart-1, case moderate — claim "Both murderers plan with care, feel the victim's heart, satisfy the law and deny they are mad. (쌍: '…by pulsation the exact locality of the heart' ↔ 'I placed my hand upon the heart and held it there many minutes. There was no pulsation.'; 'All the other evidence corresponded—the door locked inside, the position of the corpse, the burned papers.' ↔ 'The officers were satisfied. My manner had convinced them.'; 'They say now that I am mad; but they are mistaken.' ↔ 'but why will you say that I am mad?'). 실패한 lead 없음. 남긴 leads: The Pioneer 같은 호의 무서명 글 각주('his story of the “Tell-tale heart,” on page 29')는 저자 미상이라 context 아님; Aristidean 1845-10의 Black Cat 서평(무서명)은 G17-02 몫; Pioneer·Broadway Journal·Raven 판 대조.

But now his own heart beats calmly, and the sound is the walled-up cat's scream, which every officer hears; they open the wall without his confession." (쌍: 'My heart beat calmly as that of one who slumbers in innocence.' ↔ 'It is the beating of his hideous heart!'; 'I rapped heavily, with a cane … behind which stood the corpse of the wife of my bosom.' ↔ 'placed my own seat upon the very spot beneath which reposed the corpse of the victim.'; '…than I was answered by a voice from within the tomb!' ↔ '“Villains!” I shrieked, “dissemble no more! I admit the deed!'). ② note Strange Case of Dr Jekyll and Mr Hyde answers The Black Cat(lead '자기 안의 악을 고백' 확인; contact none) / r:jekyll-and-hyde-black-cat-1, case moderate — claim "Both confessions, written at the edge of death, trace a murder to an evil called primitive in man and set loose by drink. (쌍: 'the thorough and primitive duality of man' ↔ 'perverseness is one of the primitive impulses of the human heart'; 'My devil had been long caged, he came out roaring.' ↔ '…a more than fiendish malevolence, gin-nurtured, thrilled every fibre of my frame.'; 'Will Hyde die upon the scaffold? …' ↔ 'But to-morrow I die, and to-day I would unburthen my soul.'). ③ note Notes from Underground answers The Black Cat(task lead 아님 — 'perverseness' 구절로 뷰를 훑어 찾음; contact none, Vremya 1861의 번역은 T0 → leads) / r:notes-from-underground-black-cat-1, case moderate — claim "Both narrators name a wish that philosophy leaves out: to choose, knowingly, what harms oneself. (쌍: '…man may consciously, purposely, desire what is injurious to himself…' ↔ 'Who has not, a hundred times, found himself committing a vile or a silly action…'; '…against which all systems and theories are continually being shattered to atoms.' ↔ 'Of this spirit philosophy takes no account.'; '…what do I care for the laws of nature and arithmetic…' ↔ '…to violate that which is Law, merely because we understand it to be such?'). 실패한 lead 없음. 남긴 leads: Aristidean의 앞 호 혹평('In our last number')은 archive.org에 없음(1.3 = 1845-08에는 언급 없음); 지하실 벽 속 매장('as the monks of the middle ages…')은 G17-03 Cask와 함께 heading 후보; 1843 신문판·1845 Tales·Raven 판 대조.

(쌍: 'They belong to a merchant who was left behind' ↔ 'but by mischance he was left behind upon a desert island …'; 'It shall be inscribed in the archives of my kingdom and published everywhere abroad.' ↔ 'he ordered my adventures to be written in letters of gold and laid up in the archives of his kingdom'; '"I will hear it some other time," replied the princess.' ↔ 'I told my story, concealing nothing'). 과제 lead(shares-form frame)는 그대로는 실패: 우리 Camaralzaman 원문엔 Scheherazade 액자도 밤 나눔도 없고 Sindbad의 액자(짐꾼 Hindbad에게 잔치에서 들려줌)에 대응하는 것이 없음 — 대신 구절로 확인된 공유 장치(배가 두고 떠난 상인의 짐, 낯선 이의 이야기를 왕이 기록고에 남김)로 note를 냄. ② note The Man Who Would Be King answers Camaralzaman(leads 밖 후보, 원문으로 확인) / r:man-who-would-be-king-camaralzaman-1 — claim "In both a stranger made king of a foreign people must take a wife there, and the wedding threatens to show what the king is.
