(쌍: 'in which the foulness of the air caused our flambeaux rather to glow than flame.' ↔ 'our torches, half smothered in its oppressive atmosphere, gave us little opportunity for investigation'; 'that I might hearken to it with the more satisfaction, I ceased my labors and sat down upon the bones.' ↔ 'I now tell you that I heard her first feeble movements in the hollow coffin.'; 'For the half of a century no mortal has disturbed them.' ↔ 'Madman! I tell you that she now stands without the door!'). 실패한 lead 없음. 남긴 leads: Stevenson 서평은 1875년까지 Ingram 판 단편집을 읽었다는 기록이라 jekyll-and-hyde answers black-cat의 contact를 올릴 encounter(B2) 후보(다만 Black Cat 이름은 없음); 서평의 Masque of the Red Death(Prospero 연회의 시계) 대목은 G25 Masque의 context 후보; Godey's 1846·Griswold 1850·Raven 판 대조.

(쌍: 'she determined to feign madness' ↔ 'You have lost your senses and you must be treated accordingly.'; 'as the cure depended neither on them nor their skill, but only on the princess herself' ↔ 'This was the first of many suitors for the princess whose inability to cure her cost them their lives.'; 'Look at me, princess, and you will see that I am no doctor, but the Prince of Persia, who has come to set you free.' ↔ 'As regards my person, your Majesty has rightly guessed that I am not an astrologer.'). rewrites가 아니라 answers: 같은 모음집 안 두 이야기의 선후를 알 수 없어 누가 누구 장면을 가져왔는지 말할 수 없음. ② note The Time Machine answers The Enchanted Horse(lead 확인) / r:time-machine-enchanted-horse-1 — claim "Both tales lose a saddled machine with paired controls to people who store it without knowing its use, and its rider wins it back by mounting it among them. The Time Traveller built it and keeps its levers; Firouz flies before learning its screws and recovers it only by a sham." (쌍: 'this lever, being pressed over, sends the machine gliding into the future, and this other reverses the motion' ↔ 'he discovered a tiny little peg, much smaller than the other, close to the right ear'; 'I have suspected since that the Morlocks had even partially taken it to pieces while trying in their dim way to grasp its purpose.' ↔ 'he had ordered the enchanted horse to be taken to his treasury as a curiosity, though he was quite ignorant how it could be used'; 'But at last the lever was fixed and pulled over.' ↔ 'Springing lightly up behind the lady, he leaned forward and turned the peg'). ③ note A Connecticut Yankee answers The Enchanted Horse(leads 밖 후보, 원문으로 확인) / r:connecticut-yankee-enchanted-horse-1 — claim "In both a stranger promises to break a spell before a seated ruler and a crowd, with smoke and meaningless words. Hank's show is the point and wins him the crowd while a pump does the work; Firouz invents the spell, and its smoke covers his flight with the princess." (쌍: 'Behold, in another minute the spell will be broken, or no mortal can break it.' ↔ 'a portion of its enchantment has by some means been communicated to her person, and it can only be dissipated by certain perfumes of which I possess the secret'; 'When you are going to do a miracle for an ignorant race, you want to get in every detail that will count' ↔ 'give the court and the people one of the most astonishing spectacles they have ever witnessed'; 'then slowly pronounced this ghastly word with a kind of awfulness which caused hundreds to tremble' ↔ 'walked three times round the horse, muttering the while certain words'). leads 판정: Camaralzaman 통과 → ①, Time Machine 통과 → ②. Sindbad 실패 — Firouz가 '산과 들을 분간할 수 없을 때까지' 오르는 장면과 Sindbad가 roc에 매달려 '땅이 보이지 않을 때까지' 오르는 장면은 맞지만, 다룰 수 없는 것에 실려 하늘로 가는 것은 모티프 공유일 뿐이고(Gulliver의 독수리도 같음, 대체 테스트 실패), 우리 원문엔 액자가 없어 액자 후보도 실패. 떨어뜨린 후보: heading:holy-mans-disguise(Firouz가 데르비시 옷을 입지만 원문은 그 옷이 적의 눈을 피하게 했다고 하지 않음, 들어가게 한 것은 의사 옷). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로)

Andersen's mermaid pays a witch her voice for a human body, hoping a man's love will give her a soul; Wilde's Fisherman pays a witch a dance to be rid of his soul and live in the sea. She buys feet to dance for the prince; he leaves the footless Mermaid for a dancer's feet." (쌍: '‘Thou hast a human soul,’ … ‘If only thou wouldst send away thy soul, then could I love thee.’' ↔ 'then his soul would glide into your body'; '‘Thou must dance with me, pretty boy,’ she murmured' ↔ 'this voice you must give to me'; 'he remembered that the little Mermaid had no feet and could not dance' ↔ 'though each time her foot touched the floor it seemed as if she trod on sharp knives'). ② note The Fisherman and His Soul answers The Picture of Dorian Gray(lead 확인, 같은 작가) / r:fisherman-and-his-soul-dorian-gray-1 — claim "Wilde parts a man from his soul twice, and the parted soul drives him to crime. Dorian's soul is a locked-up picture that takes the marks of his sins; the Fisherman's goes out without a heart and learns evil. Each turns a knife on it: Dorian dies, but the Fisherman's knife fails, and only love lets the soul back in." (쌍: 'For her body I would give my soul, and for her love I would surrender heaven.' ↔ 'I would give my soul for that!'; '‘When thou didst send me forth into the world thou gavest me no heart, so I learned to do all these things and love them.’' ↔ 'as though it had been suggested to him by the image on the canvas, whispered into his ear by those grinning lips'; '…he strove to cut from his feet that shadow of the body which is the body of the Soul' ↔ 'As it had killed the painter, so it would kill the painter’s work'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Beauty and the Beast(영혼·마녀·바다 없음)·Dorian(소원으로 영혼을 줌, 마녀 아님)·Monkey's Paw(연인 없는 소원 값)·Frankenstein(종을 바꾸는 거래 없음)이면 거짓, Fisherman 자리는 substitute peers(Usher·Jekyll·Thursday·Goodman Brown 등)에 바다로 장가가려 영혼을 버리는 이가 없음; ②는 Jekyll(Hyde는 따로 된 몸이 직접 죄를 짓고, 영혼을 내걸지도 칼을 돌리지도 않음)·Canterville·Usher·Monkey's Paw(떼어 낸 영혼 없음)이면 거짓, Fisherman 자리는 Jekyll·Usher·Goodman Brown이면 거짓(사랑 때문에 영혼을 주지 않음). 실패한 후보 없음(leads 둘 다 원문 구절로 확인). 내지 않은 것: Canterville Ghost(소녀의 사랑으로 쉼을 얻는 유령·꽃 — 대응이 한 장면뿐, work leads), heading:wish-granted-to-ruin(소원이 비틀려 이뤄지지 않음), Speaker 1891-12-05 편지(표지·장식 이야기뿐). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).
- [x] G18-07 2026-10-09 — The Light Princess 입고(모두 proposed), `catalog.py check --stage G18-07` 0 errors 0 warnings, `test_catalog.py --stage G18-07` FAIL 0. 새 person:george-macdonald(LC n78096948 'MacDonald, George, 1824-1905' 캐시 일치 → 작가 문서 content/authors/george-macdonald.md에 person 줄 추가). work:light-princess(story, title·author ev = PG 697 'Title: The Light Princess'·'Author: George MacDonald'; first_pub 1864 ev = PG 8892 'Originally published in 1864'·Adela Cathcart 5장의 'Title: THE LIGHT PRINCESS.'), text:light-princess-pg697(PG 697은 판 이름 없음; 1864 Adela Cathcart 본문과 다른 개정본 — 'like a princess as she was' ↔ 1864 'like a princess and a philosopher', 1864의 'And then she was so disgracefully poor!' 없음 → leads). 새 heading:tears-that-will-not-come('Tears that will not come' — 주문이나 종족 때문에 울지 못하는 여주인공, 사랑을 위해 목숨이 바쳐질 때 처음 눈물이 나고 그 눈물이 그를 바꿈; G18-04 Snow Queen 후보) — index enacts: light-princess('But she never cried.' · 'All the pent-up crying of her life was spent now.'), little-mermaid('only that the mermaids have no tears, and therefore they suffer more' · 'felt them, for the first time, filling with tears'). context 1: 새 work:macdonald-adela-cathcart-frame(novel, MacDonald, Adela Cathcart vol. 1 5장 'The light princess'의 액자 발췌 3토막 […]: 낭독 전 대화와 'The story, as I now give it, is not exactly as I read it then', 낭독한 표제·Milton 모토, 낭독 뒤 청중의 말; 새 src:pg-8892, 원본 content/raw/pg8892.txt; 줄 합침·밑줄 제거·'--'→대시 외 손대지 않음, 정정 없음) → light-princess, role composition, 근거 'Adela, if you and our friends have no objection, I will read you a story I have just scribbled off.' · 'that no girl is worth anything till she has cried a little'. gloss 없음(왕비의 Hamlet 인용 'this effect defective came by cause'는 라이브러리 밖). 승인용 note·reading(둘 다 answers, contact none, case moderate, 3쌍, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·단편·중편 98편 → passed): ① note The Light Princess answers The Little Mermaid(lead 확인, 판정은 rewrites parody가 아니라 answers) / r:light-princess-little-mermaid-1 — claim "MacDonald turns Andersen's sea tale round. Andersen's tearless mermaid saves a drowning prince unthanked and dies for him. MacDonald's prince 'saves' a princess who was only swimming, then drowns stopping her lake though she does not love him; she cannot keep his head above water, and her first tears bring him back and make her human." (쌍: 'he brought her to shore in a fashion ignominious to a swimmer' ↔ 'so she swam with the handsome prince to the beach'; 'But she never cried.' ↔ 'only that the mermaids have no tears, and therefore they suffer more'; 'She got hold of him, and held his head above the water' ↔ 'She held his head above the water, and let the waves drift them where they would.'). ② note The Light Princess answers Beauty and the Beast(새 후보) / r:light-princess-beauty-and-the-beast-1 — claim "MacDonald moves the spell from the lover to the beloved. The Beast lies dying while Beauty forgets her promise, and her water and her avowal give him back his shape. The prince drowns by his own will for a princess who forgot him; she drags him from the water, and her grief breaks her own enchantment." (쌍: 'As for the prince, she had forgotten him.' ↔ 'I was dying because I thought you had forgotten your promise.'; 'Love and water brought back all her strength.' ↔ 'she sprinkled it over his face, and, to her great delight, he began to revive'; 'My darling child! she's found her gravity!' ↔ 'I never knew how much I loved you until just now, when I feared I was too late to save your life.'). 실패한 lead 판정: Little Mermaid rewrites(parody)는 통과 못 함 — MacDonald는 Andersen의 줄거리(마녀와의 거래, 목소리, 다른 신부, 칼)를 하나도 가져오지 않고 작품·작가 이름도 부르지 않음. 10장의 'we must turn merman and mermaid'는 물속에서만 제 모습인 아내에 대한 왕자의 농담이고, 이야기가 이름으로 비꼬는 대상은 옛 동화의 세례식 저주('She despised all the modes we read of in history, in which offended fairies and witches have taken their revenges') → 같은 장면을 역할을 뒤집어 다르게 답하는 answers로 냄. 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Frankenstein(강에서 소녀를 건지지만 Creature는 울고, 사랑받는 쪽이 아님)·Beauty and the Beast(물 구조 없음, Beauty는 잘 욺)이면 거짓; ②는 Jane Eyre(잊은 적 없는 Rochester에게 돌아감)·Little Mermaid(사랑하는 이가 왕자를 되살리지 않음)·Werther(되살아나지 않음)이면 거짓. 내지 않은 것: Gulliver's Travels(Hum-Drum·Kopy-Keck의 사혈·공기 펌프·생매장 처방이 Lagado 계획가 풍자와 닮았으나 구절 대조 안 함 — 뒤 패스 후보), heading:wish-granted-to-ruin(연처럼 날고 싶다는 소원은 이뤄지지 않고, 저주는 복수지 소원이 아님) — work leads에 적음. site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

(쌍: 'then she took off her red shoes, the most precious things she possessed, and threw them both into the river' ↔ 'the best thing you possess will I have for the price of my draught'; 'I can give her no more power than what she has already' ↔ 'I will prepare a draught for you'; 'Then little Gerda shed burning tears; and they fell on his bosom' ↔ 'the mermaid sighed deeply, but she could not shed tears'). ② note Dorian Gray answers Snow Queen(lead 확인, 방향은 1890 → 1844, contact none) / r:dorian-gray-snow-queen-1, case moderate — claim "Andersen's sprite makes a mirror that turns the good ugly, and its splinter freezes Kay's heart until Gerda's tears wash it out. (쌍: 'This portrait would be to him the most magical of mirrors' ↔ 'he had made a mirror with the power of causing all that was good and beautiful … to look poor and mean'; 'flinging the mirror on the floor, crushed it into silver splinters beneath his heel' ↔ 'it flew out of their hands and fell to the earth, where it was dashed in a hundred million and more pieces'; 'Some love might come across his life, and purify him' ↔ 'they thawed the lumps of ice, and consumed the splinters of the looking-glass'). 내지 않은 후보: Beauty and the Beast(소녀의 사랑이 변한 이를 되돌린다는 줄거리만 같음 — Lang 판에는 눈물도 거울도 없고 Beast는 혼인 약속으로 돌아옴, 대체 테스트가 Little Mermaid로도 넘어감), heading:the-witchs-price(Gerda는 다른 종으로 건너가지 않고 마녀가 값을 받지 않음 — 범위 밖). (쌍: 'scratch for fleas and pretend to be men' ↔ 'Being naturally great mimics of men’s actions …'; 'the falling of a nut turns their minds to laughter and all is forgotten' ↔ 'The Monkeys at the sight of the nuts forgot their dancing'; 'The monkeys called the place their city …' ↔ Fox and Monkey 'are erected in honour of my ancestors …'). lead(짐승의 법)는 법 쪽이 아니라 Dancing Monkeys·Fox and the Monkey로 확인됨 — 우리 77편에 짐승이 배우거나 외우는 법이 없음. ② note Island of Doctor Moreau answers Kaa's Hunting / r:island-of-doctor-moreau-kaas-hunting-1 — lead는 Kaa → Moreau였으나 Moreau(1896)가 뒤 작품이라 방향을 Moreau → Kaa로 둠. claim "Kipling's Law of the Jungle is words a man-cub learns by heart so that beasts will help him; the monkeys who only steal words are outcasts, and a beating settles all scores. (쌍: 'This Law they were ever repeating, I found, and ever breaking.' ↔ 'he grew very tired of saying the same thing over a hundred times'; Ape-man 'would praise it very much, ask me to say it again, learn it by heart' ↔ 'use the stolen words which they overhear when they listen'; 'Evil are the punishments of those who break the Law. (쌍: 'I wanted to damage every man in the place, and every woman—and not in their bodies or in their estate, but in their vanity' ↔ 'after such a refusal of hospitality as I have just experienced, you will not be surprised if that visit is the last I ever pay you'; 'There wasn’t any pauper stranger, nor any twenty-dollar contribution' ↔ 'the water which has been refused to the cry of the weary and dying is unholy'; 'Friends, they are only gilded disks of lead!' ↔ 'we can put a great deal of copper into the gold without anyone's finding it out'). lead 판정: Two Brothers는 원문 구절로 확인돼 note로 냈으나 lead의 틀('착한 막내와 못된 형들')은 Grimm 원문에 없음(악인은 쌍둥이의 부자 삼촌 금세공인, 쌍둥이는 둘 다 착함) → 다른 구절(쇠꼬챙이 구이, 추운 노인의 청, 돌이 된 형제)로 냄. 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Beauty and the Beast·Rip Van Winkle·Monkey's Paw, substitute peers(Jekyll·Dorian·Usher·Fisherman 등)에 쇠꼬챙이 구이·돌이 된 형제가 없어 거짓; ②는 peers(Million Pound Bank-Note·The Bet)·Young Goodman Brown·Monkey's Paw에 문전박대된 낯선 이의 복수·자선과 묶인 금이 없어 거짓. 내지 않은 것: Selected Fables(우화풍이나 구절 대응 없음), Monkey's Paw·heading:wish-granted-to-ruin(Gluck의 소원은 왕이 거절함). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

Kay is frozen by a splinter and a queen's kiss, cannot say the Lord's Prayer, and Gerda's tears thaw him. Helga is born cold; a foster-mother's kiss on the frog's forehead and Christ's name, found at last on her own lips, set her free." (쌍: 'But love never entered thy heart; it is cold and clammy, like the plants on the moor.' ↔ 'she had kissed away all feeling of cold from his body, and his heart was a lump of ice'; 'she pressed a kiss on the forehead of the hideous frog-child' ↔ 'and then she kissed his forehead. Ah! it was colder than ice'; 'rose to her lips, and she uttered the words' ↔ 'he tried to repeat the Lord's Prayer; but all he could do, he was only able to remember the multiplication table'). ③ note The Marsh King's Daughter answers Rip Van Winkle(새 후보) / r:marsh-kings-daughter-rip-van-winkle-1 — claim "Andersen turns Irving's homecoming into a death. Rip, who slept among ghosts, takes his seat again at the inn door; Helga, who lingered to look at heaven, has no place left and falls to dust." (쌍: 'Three minutes only in heaven, and a whole night on earth had passed away!' ↔ 'the whole twenty years had been to him but as one night'; 'she vanished on the evening of her wedding day, many hundred years ago, and never came back' ↔ 'it’s twenty years since he went away from home with his gun, and never has been heard of since'; 'The body crumbled into dust, and a faded lotus-flower lay on the spot on which Helga had stood.' ↔ 'he took his place once more on the bench, at the inn door'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Dorian(영혼이 그림이지 둘째 몸이 아님)·Fisherman(영혼이 따로 걸어 나감)·Usher(분신이 누이)·Beauty and the Beast(본성 하나)이면 거짓, Jekyll 자리에 다른 몸으로 깨어나는 작품 없음; ②는 Little Mermaid(차가운 마음·이마 입맞춤 없음)·Beauty and the Beast(거룩한 말 없음)·Rappaccini(독이지 사랑 없음 아님)이면 거짓; ③은 Connecticut Yankee(사라졌다는 말을 듣지 않음)·Time Machine(같은 날 저녁 돌아옴)·Sleepy Hollow(돌아오지 않음)이면 거짓, 돌아옴이 죽음으로 끝나는 작품은 이 작품뿐. 실패한 lead 없음(둘 다 원문 구절로 확인). 내지 않은 것: Beauty and the Beast·heading:loved-though-hideous(개구리는 사랑을 청하지 않고, 풀림은 혼인 약속이 아니라 그리스도의 이름과 꿈속 입맞춤 — 줄거리만 닮음), Little Mermaid(목소리 잃음·하늘로 오름이 닮았으나 행위 단위로 맞는 구절 없음) — work leads에 적음. site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

(쌍: 'There ought to be a Princess. (쌍: '“I have no stick—I have no stick,” said Kim. “I will get me one and break his back.”' ↔ 'while Teddy’s father beat the dead Karait'; 'He is upon the Wheel as we are …' ↔ '“Well,” said Rikki-tikki, … “marks or no marks'; 'He passed within a foot of the cobra’s poised head. In English. Then in French.'의 머리말 두 문단과 끝맺음 한 문단, 영어 본문·프랑스어 번역·역번역은 […]) → jumping-frog, role preface(ev 'he says my Jumping Frog is a funny story, but still he can't see why it should ever really convulse any one with laughter' · 'When I say, “Well, I don't see no p'ints about that frog …” is it kind, is it just'; locus 낯선 이의 'I don’t see no p’ints about that frog'). gloss 없음(Daniel Webster·Andrew Jackson은 개구리·개 이름일 뿐). 승인용 note·reading(모두 contact none, 각 3쌍, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·단편 110편 → 모두 passed): ① note Jumping Frog shares-form(frame) Sleepy Hollow(lead 확인) / r:jumping-frog-sleepy-hollow-1, case moderate — claim "Both tales end in a frame where a sober man weighs a tall tale told aloud. Irving gives the grave face to a listener who demands the moral; his teller believes half of it. Twain gives the gravity to the teller, who admires his heroes in earnest, and the listener, finding no use in it, walks out mid-yarn." (쌍: 'He never smiled, he never frowned …' ↔ 'maintained a grave and rather severe face throughout'; 'admired its two heroes as men of transcendent genius in finesse' ↔ 'I don’t believe one-half of it myself'; 'I did not think that a continuation … and so I started away.' ↔ 'what was the moral of the story, and what it went to prove?'). ② note Connecticut Yankee shares-form(frame) Jumping Frog(lead 아님, 같은 작가) / r:connecticut-yankee-jumping-frog-1, case moderate — claim "Twain builds the Yankee's frame from the frog's: a stranger met by chance does all the talking and tells of wonders as flatly as of the weather. Wheeler's monotone bores the narrator, who walks out before the end; the Warwick stranger's spell holds his, who waits up by the fire and plies him with whisky to hear the rest." (쌍: 'for he did all the talking' ↔ 'Simon Wheeler backed me into a corner …'; 'just as one might speak of the weather' ↔ 'he never betrayed the slightest suspicion of enthusiasm'; 'hot Scotch whisky … hoping always for his story' ↔ 'I did not wait to hear about the afflicted cow'). ③ note Hadleyburg rewrites(transposition) Jumping Frog(lead 아님, 같은 작가) / r:hadleyburg-jumping-frog-1, case speculative — claim "In both, a stranger passing through brings down a local boast with lead. The camp stranger fills Smiley's champion frog with quail shot, takes forty dollars and is never caught. Hadleyburg's stranger, nursing an offence for a year, fills a sack with gilded lead disks, and the town's pride in its honesty breaks open in its own hall." (쌍: 'they are only gilded disks of lead!' ↔ 'he belched out a double handful of shot'; 'offend a passing stranger' ↔ 'a stranger in the camp'; 'prouder of it than of any other of its possessions' ↔ 'Smiley was monstrous proud of his frog'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Rip Van Winkle(여관에서 의심받지만 엄숙한 청자·반만 믿는 화자 없음)·Heart of Darkness·Lord Jim(청자가 끝까지 들음)·Adela Cathcart·Mugby Junction 틀(교훈을 묻지 않음)에서 거짓; ②는 Sleepy Hollow(화자가 'pleasant'하고 끝까지 함)·Rip(액자 화자가 그를 만나지 않음)에서 거짓; ③은 £1,000,000 Bank-Note(내기에 속임수·납 없음)·Gold-Bug(보물이 진짜)에서 거짓. 내지 않은 것: Rip Van Winkle을 note로(여관에서 의심받는 이야기 모티프뿐, 화자의 무표정·떠나는 청자 구절 없음 → index만), £1,000,000 Bank-Note(신사들의 실험 내기, 속임수에 진 노름꾼 아님), Aesop·Kaa's Hunting(말하거나 훈련된 짐승 — Dan'l Webster는 말하지도 사람 행세도 안 함). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

Where are you? Where have you been? How came you here?') → refers note 자동. Ali Baba·Valentine and Orson·Sultan's Groom·Hamlet은 그래프 작품 아님. context 1: 새 work:dickens-christmas-carol-1843-preface(preface, 'C. D. December, 1843.', 원문 content/contexts/dickens-christmas-carol-1843-preface.txt = PG 46 서문 전문) → christmas-carol, role preface(ev 'to raise the Ghost of an Idea, which shall not put my readers out of humour with themselves' · 'May it haunt their houses pleasantly, and no one wish to lay it.'). 승인용 note·reading(모두 lead 확인, contact none, case moderate, 각 3쌍, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·중편·단편 110편 → 모두 passed): ① note The Signal-Man answers A Christmas Carol / r:signal-man-christmas-carol-1 — claim "Dickens sends each man a faceless figure that foreshows a death, and each asks whether it can be averted. Scrooge's Phantom points him to his own grave while there is time, and he wakes to change the end. The signalman's spectre names no place or remedy, and its gesture proves to be the driver's at his death." (쌍: 'I never saw the face. The left arm is across the face, and the right arm is waved.' ↔ 'It was shrouded in a deep black garment, … save one outstretched hand.'; 'Why not tell me how it could be averted—if it could have been averted?' ↔ 'Are these the shadows of the things that Will be, or are they shadows of things that May be, only?'; 'I put this arm before my eyes, not to see, and I waved this arm to the last; but it was no use.' ↔ 'read upon the stone of the neglected grave his own name, EBENEZER SCROOGE.'). ② note The Haunted and the Haunters answers A Christmas Carol / r:haunted-and-the-haunters-christmas-carol-1 — claim "Both books bring a ghost to a man who first explains it away. Marley speaks at length, wears the chain his life forged, and comes to save his partner's soul. Bulwer's theorist holds that the true soul cannot be called back: what appears is an earth-stained phantom raised by a living brain, and it comes for little or no object." (쌍: 'must originate in some human being gifted by constitution with the power so to present them' ↔ 'A slight disorder of the stomach makes them cheats.'; 'a phantom of what has been most earth-stained on earth' ↔ 'I wear the chain I forged in life'; 'if they speak, they utter no ideas above those of an ordinary person on earth' ↔ 'I am here to-night to warn you, that you have yet a chance and hope of escaping my fate.'). ③ note A Christmas Carol answers Oliver Twist / r:christmas-carol-oliver-twist-1 — claim "Oliver Twist shows the workhouse from inside, through a boy the board starves and a white-waistcoated gentleman dooms to the gallows. The Carol puts the board's creed in a rich man's mouth: Scrooge sends the poor to prisons and workhouses. Then a Spirit quotes him back over Tiny Tim and two starved children, and he repents." (쌍: 'And the Union workhouses?' ↔ 'of being starved by a gradual process in the house, or by a quick one out of it'; 'If he be like to die, he had better do it, and decrease the surplus population.' ↔ 'than I am that that boy will come to be hung'; 'This boy is Ignorance. This girl is Want.' ↔ 'juvenile offenders against the poor-laws'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Haunted(옛 범죄 재연)·Canterville·Turn of the Screw·Whistle·Monkey's Paw에 자기 죽음을 미리 보이는 얼굴 없는 형상·'막을 수 있나' 물음이 없어 거짓; ②는 Usher·Canterville·Turn·Signal-Man·Whistle·Dracula 등에 개심을 설교하러 오는 죽은 동업자가 없고, 반대쪽은 '영혼은 못 돌아오고 산 뇌가 불러낸 eidolon'이라는 이론이 Haunted에만 있어 거짓; ③은 Jane Eyre(학교, 교수형 아닌 지옥)·Vanka·Kim에 구빈원 이사회·교수형 예언이 없고, 가난한 이를 감옥·구빈원으로 보내라던 말을 유령이 되돌려주는 부자는 Carol에만 있어 거짓. 실패한 lead 없음. 내지 않은 것: Canterville Ghost(사슬 끄는 유령을 조롱하는 장면은 Marley와 닮았으나 구절 대응을 만들지 않음, lead 아님 → leads), Turn of the Screw(성탄 전야 유령 이야기 관습뿐). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

Towle 역, 인쇄본 미기재). 새 heading:wager-on-a-term('The wager on a term' — 신사들 자리에서 한 사람이 정해진 날수·햇수 안에 무엇을 해내거나 버틸 수 있느냐에 큰돈이 걸리고, 이야기가 그 기한을 끝까지 세며 판돈의 값을 묻는다) — index enacts: around-the-world('I will bet twenty thousand pounds against anyone who wishes that I will make the tour of the world in eighty days or less' · 'One minute more, and the wager would be won.'), million-pound-bank-note('Brother B said he would bet twenty thousand pounds that the man would live thirty days…'), the-bet('Gentlemen, I stake two millions.' · 'I shall come out from here five minutes before the stipulated term…'). gloss 없음(Sheridan·Byron·Leotard·Blondin은 라이브러리 작품 아님). context 없음(PG 103에 Verne·Towle 서문 없음, 역자 각주 1개뿐 → leads). 승인용 note·reading(모두 answers, contact none, case moderate, 각 3쌍, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·단편 113편 → 모두 passed): ① note The £1,000,000 Bank-Note answers Around the World(lead 확인) / r:million-pound-bank-note-around-the-world-1 — claim "Both tales open on twenty thousand pounds staked by English gentlemen on a man lasting a set number of days, and both close by counting a wife the real winnings. Fogg stakes his own fortune, spends it on the road and is hunted as a bank thief; Twain's stranger never spends his note and grows rich." ② note Around the World answers Twenty Thousand Leagues(lead '같은 작가' 확인) / r:around-the-world-twenty-thousand-leagues-1 — claim "Verne wrote two tours of the world. Nemo, who has done with society, roams under the sea on no schedule, and his captive guest names every town and wonder he passes. Fogg, a clubman with a timetable, crosses the same Red Sea without looking up and brings back nothing he saw, only a wife." ③ note The Bet answers Around the World(lead 아님) / r:the-bet-around-the-world-1 — claim "Chekhov's wager is Fogg's turned inside out. Fogg risks money that he can circle the globe in eighty days; the lawyer risks his freedom that he can sit fifteen years in one room. Both know the world unseen, and both bets turn on five minutes: Fogg's seemingly lost by error, the prisoner's forfeited on purpose." 내지 않은 후보(leads에 이유): Twenty Thousand Leagues shares-form(Passepartout·Conseil — 인물 묘사일 뿐 넘겨받은 장치 아님, answers가 대신함), Kim·Passage to India(영국령 인도 배경만 같고 Aouda 구출과 맞는 구절 없음), heading:sleeper-wakes(하루를 잃지만 자기를 모르는 사람들 사이로 돌아오지 않음). 기존 work leads에 줄 추가: million-pound-bank-note, the-bet, twenty-thousand-leagues.

FENIMORE COOPER’S LITERARY OFFENCES.'·'No. CCCCLXIV.'; 원문 content/contexts/twain-cooper-literary-offences.txt = PG 3172 전사, NAR 첫 인쇄본과 낱말 단위 대조 → 표기 차이만(140/one hundred and forty, toward/towards, imbedded/embedded 등) leads에) → last-of-the-mohicans, role criticism(ev 'On one occasion Alice and Cora were being chased by the French through a fog in the neighborhood of their father's fort' · 'He turned a running stream out of its course, and there, in the slush in its old bed, were that person's moccasin-tracks.' · 'the Leather Stocking Series ought to have been called the Broken Twig Series'; loci 'Point de quartier aux coquins!' · 'we shall hunt for the furrow it has made' · 'diverted its course into another channel' — Twain은 물길 돌리기를 Chingachgook에게, 우리 원문은 Uncas에게 줌, 인용의 '60ths'·'restored me my children'은 PG 940의 'Sixtieths'·'restored me to my children'과 다름 → leads); ② 새 work:cooper-mohicans-introduction(introduction, Cooper, 날짜 미입력 — 'fully a quarter of a century since' → 1850년 무렵, 1831 Bentley판 첫 게재설은 T0; 원문 content/contexts/cooper-mohicans-introduction.txt = PG 940 전사) → last-of-the-mohicans, role preface(ev 'The Mohicans were the possessors of the country first occupied by the Europeans in this portion of the continent. Both are drawn as gangling limbs on a poor mount, and both answer terror with a psalm. Ichabod's tune dies in his throat and he vanishes; Gamut sings on through a massacre, and the Hurons spare him as mad." (쌍: 'His head was large; his shoulders narrow; his arms long and dangling; while his hands were small, if not delicate.' ↔ 'He was tall, but exceedingly lank, with narrow shoulders, long arms and legs, hands that dangled a mile out of his sleeves'; 'Notwithstanding a constant application of his one armed heel to the flanks of the mare' ↔ 'He rode with short stirrups, which brought his knees nearly up to the pommel of the saddle'; 'who now appeared to the astonished natives gifted with the protecting spirit of madness' ↔ 'shutting his eyes, broke forth with involuntary fervor into a psalm tune'). ② note The Last of the Mohicans answers Rip Van Winkle(lead 확인) / r:last-of-the-mohicans-rip-van-winkle-1 — claim "Both tales show an old man of the Hudson country waking to a land with new masters. Rip sleeps twenty years, finds King George repainted as Washington, and settles on the inn bench as the village chronicle. Tamenund has lived every winter of his century, dreams Uncas's youth back, and wakes to see the last Mohican." (쌍: 'has Tamenund been a sleeper for a hundred winters?' ↔ 'I was myself last night, but I fell asleep on the mountain, and they’ve changed my gun, and everything’s changed, and I’m changed'; 'that the children of the Lenape were masters of the world' ↔ 'instead of being a subject to his Majesty George the Third, he was now a free citizen of the United States'; 'have I lived to see the last warrior of the wise race of the Mohicans' ↔ 'was reverenced as one of the patriarchs of the village, and a chronicle of the old times “before the war.”'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Moby-Dick(Bildad는 키를 잡고 찬송하지만 노래 선생이 아니고 노래 때문에 살아남지 않음)·Rip Van Winkle(교사 Van Bummel은 신문만 읽음)에 깡마른 찬송가 선생·서툰 승마·공포 앞 찬송이 없어 거짓, 반대쪽은 미친 사람으로 여겨져 살아남는 찬송가 가수가 Mohicans에만 있음; ②는 Time Machine·Connecticut Yankee(기계나 일격으로 시대를 건너지만 다시 칠한 여관 간판·마을 연대기 노인 없음)에 거짓, 반대쪽은 '백 번의 겨울을 잤는가' 묻고 자기 종족의 마지막을 보는 족장이 Mohicans에만 있음. 내지 않은 것: heading:sleeper-wakes 색인(Tamenund는 잤느냐고 묻기만 할 뿐 제 사람들 사이에서 모든 해를 살아 왔고, 떨어졌다 돌아온 사람이 아님 → note로만), Moby-Dick(Bildad의 찬송 — 구절 대응 없음), 1826 초판 Preface('The reader, who takes up these volumes' — 스캔에만 있고 PG 940엔 없음, 세 번째 context 후보로 leads에만). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

They are curious stars.'). index enacts heading:married-elsewhere(기존): hard-times(Stephen Blackpool — 'how I am to be ridded o’ this woman' · 'show me the law to help me!'). context 1: 새 work:ruskin-unto-this-last-hard-times-note(essay, Ruskin, Unto This Last 1편 'The Roots of Honour'의 각주 [31], 원문 content/contexts/ruskin-unto-this-last-hard-times-note.txt = PG 36541 각주 전문; first_pub 1862는 책 서문 'Denmark Hill, 10th May, 1862' — 1860 Cornhill 연재에 각주가 있었는지는 leads) → hard-times, role criticism(ev 'Mr. Bounderby is a dramatic monster, instead of a characteristic example of a worldly master' · 'but especially Hard Times, should be studied with close and earnest care by persons interested in social questions'; locus 'A man who was the Bully of humility.'). gloss 없음('took De Foe to their bosoms'는 작가 이름뿐이고 작품을 부르지 않음 → leads; Goldsmith·Cocker·Morgiana·Peter Piper·Tom Thumb·Slough of Despond는 그래프 작품 아님). 승인용 note·reading(둘 다 lead 확인, contact none, case moderate, 각 3쌍, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·중편·단편 111편 → 둘 다 passed): ① note Hard Times rewrites(inversion) Oliver Twist / r:hard-times-oliver-twist-1 — claim "Hard Times turns the parish orphan's story into a rich man's boast. Bounderby claims Oliver's childhood: a mother who left him, hunger, letters learnt under a thief. At the end, as in Oliver Twist, a hidden parent is brought before witnesses; but where Oliver's birth gives him a name, Mrs. Pegler proves Bounderby's misery a lie." (쌍: '‘My mother left me to my grandmother,’ said Bounderby' ↔ 'If he could have known that he was an orphan, left to the tender mercies of churchwardens and overseers, perhaps he would have cried the louder.'; 'under the direction of a drunken cripple, who was a convicted thief, and an incorrigible vagrant' ↔ 'You’d like to be able to make pocket-handkerchiefs as easy as Charley Bates, wouldn’t you, my dear?'; 'though he come of humble parents, he come of parents that loved him as dear as the best could' ↔ 'is your half-brother; the illegitimate son of your father, my dear friend Edwin Leeford, by poor young Agnes Fleming, who died in giving him birth.'). ② note The Machine Stops answers Hard Times / r:machine-stops-hard-times-1 — claim "Both books school people against seeing for themselves and shame the parent of a child who looks. Gradgrind wants facts without fancy, so no child of his sees a face in the moon. Forster's Machine wants ideas without facts: Kuno sees a man in the stars and climbs out to see them, and his mother dislikes the stars." (쌍: 'The four big stars are the man's shoulders and his knees.' ↔ 'No little Gradgrind had ever seen a face in the moon'; 'Let your ideas be second-hand, and if possible tenth-hand, for then they will be far removed from that disturbing element—direct observation.' ↔ 'Why, then, you are not to see anywhere, what you don’t see in fact; you are not to have anywhere, what you don’t have in fact.'; 'Was he really the little boy to whom she had taught the use of his stops and buttons, and to whom she had given his first lessons in the Book?' ↔ 'Thomas and you, to whom the circle of the sciences is open'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Jane Eyre(유산을 편지로 앎)·Wuthering Heights(거둬진 아이, 도둑 학교 없음)·Kim(부적으로 아버지를 앎)·Christmas Carol에 소매치기 학교와 증인들 앞에서 밝혀지는 숨은 출생이 없어 거짓, 반대쪽은 고아의 어린 시절을 자랑하다 어머니가 불려와 망신당하는 부자가 Hard Times에만 있어 거짓; ②는 Country of the Blind(하늘 이야기를 fancy라 하지만 아이를 사실로 가르치지 않음)·Flatland(제3차원 금지, 손자가 말을 거둠 — 공상 금지 교실·서커스 엿보기 없음)·Candide(교리이지 사실이 아님)·Time Machine(Eloi는 교육받지 않음)에서 거짓. 실패한 lead 없음. 내지 않은 것: Christmas Carol(Bounderby와 Scrooge는 가난한 이를 깔보는 부자이나 방문·개심이 없고 구절 쌍을 만들지 않음, lead 아님 → leads), Carlyle 헌사(PG 786에 없음 → leads). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

The book says' · 'They ain’t anybody as polite as robbers—you’ll see that in any book.'). heading:treasure-map에는 넣지 않음(Tom은 지도가 없고 'I don't want any marks'라 함). context 1: 새 work:twain-tom-sawyer-preface(preface, Twain, 'HARTFORD, 1876.', 원문 content/contexts/twain-tom-sawyer-preface.txt — PG 74 서문 세 문단, 서명·날짜는 ev) → tom-sawyer, role preface(ev 'The odd superstitions touched upon were all prevalent among children and slaves in the West…' · 'remind adults of what they once were themselves… what queer enterprises they sometimes engaged in'; locus 'They most always put in a dead man when they bury a treasure under a tree, to look out for it.'). gloss 없음(Robin Hood·'Aladdin’s Palace'·Richard III는 그래프 작품 아님). 승인용 note·reading(모두 contact none, 각 3쌍, case moderate, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·단편 113편 → 모두 passed): ① note Treasure Island answers Tom Sawyer(lead 확인; Tom Sawyer 1876이 앞이라 from Treasure Island) / r:treasure-island-tom-sawyer-1 — claim "Stevenson's hunt runs on the lore Tom recites to Huck: a yellow paper of marks, a dead man by the tree, ghosts that keep off by day. Tom has no paper and finds the gold by luck in a robber's cave; the pirates have the paper and dig an empty pit." (쌍: 'the chart on yellow paper, with the three red crosses' ↔ 'an old yellow paper that tells how to find the marks'; 'leastways, he won’t walk by day' ↔ 'ghosts don’t travel around only at night'; Ben Gunn이 보물을 동굴로 옮김 ↔ Injun Joe 'We’ll take it to my den.'). ② note Tom Sawyer answers The Gold-Bug(lead 아님) / r:tom-sawyer-gold-bug-1 — claim "Tom recites the rules of Legrand's hunt: a paper of signs to cipher out, a dead limb, a first dig that finds nothing. Legrand reads the cipher, finds the error and lifts Kidd's chest. Tom has no paper, blames witches and the hour, and gives the tree up." (쌍: 'ciphered over … signs and hy’roglyphics' ↔ 'form a cipher'; 'under the end of a limb of an old dead tree' ↔ '’tis dead limb putty much all de way.'; 'we must be in the wrong place again' ↔ 'a depth of five feet, and yet no signs of any treasure'). ③ note Tom Sawyer answers Rip Van Winkle(lead 확인) / r:tom-sawyer-rip-van-winkle-1 — claim "Both heroes toil gladly at what no one asks of them and shirk the chore they owe. Irving calls it the great error in Rip's make-up, and a scolding wife drives him to the woods. Twain makes it a law of work and play; Tom sells his fence as play, and his aunt laughs." (쌍: 'Work consists of whatever a body is obliged to do…' ↔ 'The great error in Rip’s composition…'; 'they came to jeer, but remained to whitewash.' ↔ 이웃 일·'building stone fences'; Aunt Polly 'broke into a gentle laugh' ↔ 'her tongue was incessantly going'). note로 내지 않은 후보: Robinson Crusoe·Sindbad(Jackson's Island는 마을이 보이는 놀이 야영지, 표류자의 궁리와 짝지을 구절 없음); Sleepy Hollow·Jumping Frog(같은 작가·학교 선생·유령 집은 모티프뿐, 같은 행위를 가리키는 구절 쌍 없음); heading:treasure-map(지도·암호문이 없음).
- [x] G20-06 2026-10-09 — Silas Marner 입고(모두 proposed), `catalog.py check --stage G20-06` 0 errors 0 warnings, `test_catalog.py --stage G20-06` FAIL 0. 새 person:george-eliot(LC n79045512 'Eliot, George, 1819-1880', authority 캐시 일치; content/authors/george-eliot.md에 person 줄). work:silas-marner(novel; title·author ev = PG 550 'Title: Silas Marner'·'Author: George Eliot'; first_pub 1861 Edinburgh and London, William Blackwood and Sons ev = 1861 Blackwood 한 권본 스캔 src:archive-silasmarnerweave00eliorich 'WILLIAM BLACKWOOD AND SONS'·'MDCCCLXI' — 초판 여부·4월 2일은 T0 → leads), text:silas-marner-pg550(PG 550 판 미기재, 첫 문장은 1861 스캔과 같음, 대조 안 함; Eliot 1861-09-29 일기 'Finished correcting Silas Marner'(값싼 새 판) → 어느 판인지 leads). 새 source 2: src:pg-43044(Cross, George Eliot's Life vol. II, Harper 판), src:archive-silasmarnerweave00eliorich. 새 heading:hoard-turned-to-child('The hoard turned to a child' — 돈을 세며 살던 사람이 돈을 놓치고, 남의 아이가 그 자리를 차지함) — index enacts: silas-marner('it seemed as if there were gold on the floor in front of the hearth' · 'Unlike the gold which needed nothing, and must be worshipped in close-locked solitude'), christmas-carol('Another idol has displaced me' · 'and been a spring-time in the haggard winter of his life'). context 1: 새 work:eliot-blackwood-1861-silas-marner(letter, written 1861-02-24, 원문 content/contexts/eliot-blackwood-1861-silas-marner.txt = PG 43044의 Cross 인쇄본 두 문단; Cross의 생략 여부·1885 런던 초판은 T0) → silas-marner, role letter(ev 'the remedial influences of pure, natural human relations' · 'seen a linen-weaver with a bag on his back' · 'lent itself best to metrical rather than to prose fiction'). gloss 없음(표제 제사 Wordsworth는 그래프 작품 아님, 본문의 'Christmas carol'은 Aaron이 부르는 캐럴이지 Dickens 책이 아님). 승인용 note·reading(contact none, case moderate, 각 3쌍, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·중편·단편 112편 → 둘 다 passed): ① note Silas Marner answers A Christmas Carol(lead 확인) / r:silas-marner-christmas-carol-1 — claim "Both books turn a man who lives for his coins into father to a child not his own. Scrooge chose Gain over Belle, is shown the daughter he might have had, and wakes to be Tiny Tim's second father. Silas is robbed, and a sleeping child lies where he thought he saw his gold." (쌍: 'His gold, as he hung over it and saw it grow, gathered his power of loving together into a hard isolation like its own.' ↔ 'until the master-passion, Gain, engrosses you'; 'instead of the hard coin with the familiar resisting outline, his fingers encountered soft warm curls' ↔ 'such another creature, quite as graceful and as full of promise, might have called him father'; 'the child was come instead of the gold—that the gold had turned into the child' ↔ 'to Tiny Tim, who did NOT die, he was a second father.'). ② note Silas Marner answers Oliver Twist(lead 아님) / r:silas-marner-oliver-twist-1 — claim "Both books begin a child's life beside a vagrant mother dead after a long walk, the child bound for the parish. Dickens's surgeon sees no ring; the ring is stolen from the corpse and at last sunk in the river. Eliot's dead woman wears hers, and a weaver keeps the child and the ring." (쌍: 'She’s got a wedding-ring on, however. They must fetch her away to the workhouse to-morrow.' ↔ 'no wedding-ring, I see'; 'Will they make me take her?' ↔ 'left to the tender mercies of churchwardens and overseers'; 'carefully preserved by him in a little lackered box shaped like a shoe' ↔ 'dropped it into the stream'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Oliver Twist(Fagin의 보석 상자, 아이가 그 자리를 차지하지 않음)·King of the Golden River·Hadleyburg(금은 탐욕 시험일 뿐)·Hard Times(Gradgrind가 Sissy를 거두지만 수전노 아님)·Oh, Whistle에 거짓, 반대쪽은 Gain에 밀려난 딸을 보고 '두 번째 아버지'가 되는 수전노가 Carol에만; ②는 Jane Eyre·Wuthering Heights(거둬진 고아, 길에서 죽은 어머니·반지·교구 없음)·Kim(아버지 서류를 부적에 넣지만 어머니 시신·교구 없음)·Hard Times(Sissy는 살아 있는 아버지가 버림)·Christmas Carol에 거짓. 실패한 lead 없음. 내지 않은 것: Wuthering Heights(데려온 업둥이 모티프뿐), Cross Life의 1860-11-28 일기·1861-03-19 Bray 편지(두 번째 context 후보 → leads). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).
- [x] G22-05 2026-10-09 — The Phantom of the Opera 입고(모두 proposed), `catalog.py check --stage G22-05` 0 errors 0 warnings, `test_catalog.py --stage G22-05` FAIL 0. work:phantom-of-the-opera(novel, lang fr, 새 person:gaston-leroux LC n50050425 'Leroux, Gaston, 1868-1927' 일치 → 작가 문서에 person 줄 추가; title·author ev = PG 175 'Title: The Phantom of the Opera'·'Author: Gaston Leroux'; first_pub 1910은 서가 연도 — Le Gaulois 연재·Lafitte 책·영역 연도는 T0 → leads), text:phantom-of-the-opera-pg175(역자 이름 없음, 'Translator's Note' 각주 하나, 인쇄본 미기재). index enacts heading:loved-though-hideous(기존 heading; 'All I wanted was to be loved for myself.' · 'it is a corpse that loves you and adores you and will never, never leave you!'). gloss 없음(Faust·Romeo and Juliet·Othello·Don Juan은 라이브러리 작품 아님; 'Red Death'는 Poe 'Masque of the Red Death'를 가리키지만 이 단계 뷰에 없음 → leads). context 없음(PG 175에 Leroux 서문 없음 — Prologue는 본문, 역자 각주 하나뿐 → leads). 승인용 note·reading(contact none, case moderate, 각 3쌍, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·단편 118편 → 둘 다 passed): ① note The Phantom of the Opera rewrites Beauty and the Beast(inversion, lead 확인) / r:phantom-of-the-opera-beauty-and-the-beast-1 — claim "Leroux runs the tale backwards. Like the Beast, Erik keeps a girl in his hidden house, begs her love, and lets her go with a ring and a promise to return. Beauty's return makes the Beast her dream prince; Christine's kiss frees her to marry Raoul, and she will return only to bury Erik." (쌍: 'All I wanted was to be loved for myself.' ↔ 'Do you love me, Beauty? Will you marry me?'; 'I give you back your liberty, Christine, on condition that this ring is always on your finger.' ↔ 'turn this ring round upon your finger and say firmly'; 'I made Christine swear to come back, one night, when I was dead' ↔ 'I was dying because I thought you had forgotten your promise.'). ② note The Phantom of the Opera answers The Picture of Dorian Gray(lead 확인) / r:phantom-of-the-opera-dorian-gray-1 — claim "Both men tear off a cover, force someone who loved them to look at the hidden face, and hold that the sight binds the viewer for life. Dorian's fair face is the mask, and he kills the painter; Erik's ugliness is no mask but his own flesh, and he weeps and keeps the singer." (쌍: 'Feast your eyes, glut your soul on my cursed ugliness!' ↔ 'Now you shall look on it face to face.'; 'Perhaps you think that I have another mask, eh, and that this ... this ... my head is a mask?' ↔ 'It is the face of my soul.'; 'who have torn off my mask and who therefore can never leave me again!' ↔ 'was to be burdened for the rest of his life with the hideous memory of what he had done.'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Frankenstein(짝을 요구, 반지 없음)·Jane Eyre(돌아옴에 반지·약속 없음)·Little Mermaid·Marsh King's Daughter에 반지로 내보내고 돌아올 약속에 목숨이 걸린 흉한 구혼자가 없어 거짓; ②는 Jekyll and Hyde(약으로 몸이 바뀜, 덮개를 찢지 않음)·Minister's Black Veil(베일을 벗지 않음)·Birth-Mark·Frankenstein에 덮개를 찢어 숨긴 얼굴을 보이고 본 사람을 평생 묶는 장면이 없어 거짓. 내지 않은 것: Frankenstein(사랑받고 싶은 괴물·어머니가 준 가면 — creature는 만든 이에게 짝을 요구, 구절 대응이 heading 수준을 넘지 않음 → heading 공유로만). Beauty and the Beast·Dorian Gray 노드 leads에 새 note 한 줄씩 더함. site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).
- [x] G21-06 2026-10-09 — Adventures of Huckleberry Finn 입고(모두 proposed), `catalog.py check --stage G21-06` 0 errors 0 warnings, `test_catalog.py --stage G21-06` FAIL 0. work:huckleberry-finn(novel, person:mark-twain 기존; title·author ev = PG 76 'Title: Adventures of Huckleberry Finn'·'Author: Mark Twain'; first_pub 1884는 서가 연도, PG 76에 날짜 없음 → 런던 1884·뉴욕 1885 판은 T0 leads), text:huckleberry-finn-pg76(판 이름 없음; 우리 원문은 차례·삽화 목록·Notice·Explanatory를 빼고 CHAPTER I부터). index enacts: heading:playing-by-the-book('Don’t I tell you it’s in the books? …' · 'the way all the best authorities does is to saw the bed-leg in two'). context 1: 새 work:twain-huck-finn-notice(preface, Twain, 원문 content/contexts/twain-huck-finn-notice.txt — PG 76의 Notice와 Explanatory, 서명은 ev) → huckleberry-finn, role preface(ev 'persons attempting to find a moral in it will be banished' · 'the ordinary “Pike County” dialect…'; locus 'My folks was living in Pike County, in Missouri, where I was born'). 승인용: ① gloss:huckleberry-finn-tom-sawyer(alludes, lead 확인) → refers note Huckleberry Finn → Tom Sawyer — 첫 문장 'You don’t know about me without you have read a book by the name of The Adventures of Tom Sawyer', see 'The sum amounted to a little over twelve thousand dollars.' ② note Huckleberry Finn rewrites Tom Sawyer(continuation, 같은 작가, case strong) / r:huckleberry-finn-tom-sawyer-1 — claim "Huck starts where Tom Sawyer, a book he says told the truth 'mainly', winds up: the cave gold, the widow, and the robber gang Tom promised. Tom Sawyer ends on that bargain; the sequel spends it. The gang's raid finds a Sunday-school picnic, Huck resigns within a month, and he tries to give the gold away." (쌍: 'We got six thousand dollars apiece—all gold.' ↔ 'The sum amounted to a little over twelve thousand dollars.'; 'I might join if I would go back to the widow and be respectable' ↔ 'Well, I’ll go back to the widder for a month…'; 'as polite as pie to them; and by-and-by they fall in love with you' ↔ 'the women get to loving you…'). ③ note Kim answers Huckleberry Finn(lead 확인, contact none, case moderate; Huck 1884가 앞이라 from Kim) / r:kim-huckleberry-finn-1 — claim "Each boy travels with an elder seeking freedom: Jim from slavery, the lama from the Wheel. Huck's raft hides from people; Kim's road is a river of them. Huck is glad to be named Tom Sawyer; Kim asks who Kim is. A will frees Jim at home; the lama's River is at his feet." (쌍: 'a vast improvement on the cramped and crowded Lahore streets' ↔ 'Other places do seem so cramped up and smothery, but a raft don’t.'; 'No; I am Kim. … Who is Kim?' ↔ 'it was like being born again, I was so glad to find out who I was.'; 'I have wrenched my Soul back from the Threshold of Freedom to free thee from all sin' ↔ 'she set him free in her will'). 대체 테스트: begin 뷰 소설·단편 116편 → 둘 다 passed(Tom Sawyer 자리: Treasure Island·Oliver Twist에 동굴 금·과부·약속된 도적단 없음; Kim 자리: Treasure Island·Lord Jim·Man Who Would Be King·Passage to India에 자유를 찾는 노인을 안내하는 소년·붐비는 길 대 답답한 마을 없음; Huck 자리: Tom Sawyer에 자유를 찾는 동행·빌린 이름 없음). 실패한 lead: Last of the Mohicans — 우리 원문이 Cooper·Mohicans·Uncas·Natty를 부르지 않음('sweat like an Injun'뿐), note 없음; Walter Scott — 'the Walter Scott'은 난파 증기선 이름이고 Scott 작품을 부르지 않으며 Scott 소설은 이 단계 그래프에 없음(scott-blackwoods-1818은 Frankenstein 서평), gloss·note 없음. gloss 안 함: Don Quixote·Pilgrim's Progress(G35 몫), Baron Trenck·Casanova·Cellini·Henri IV·Iron Mask·Hamlet(그래프 작품 아님), 램프·반지 지니(Aladdin, 그래프에 없음). 내지 않은 것: Robinson Crusoe·Sindbad(Jackson's Island, 짝지을 구절 없음). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

(쌍: 'somewhat to embellish so many of my own little adventures' ↔ 'You have attempted to tinge it with romanticism'; 'My life is spent in one long effort to escape from the commonplaces of existence.' ↔ 'But I abhor the dull routine of existence.'; Flaubert 인용 ↔ 'I claim no credit in such cases. (쌍: 'Abbots and Archery and Armour and Architecture and Attica' ↔ 'As if long famishing for something to copy…'; 'I never dared to leave the room for an instant' ↔ 'he never went to dinner; indeed that he never went any where.'; 'He said that he had never heard of any such body.' ↔ 'you are responsible for the man you left there'). ③ note The £1,000,000 Bank-Note answers The Red-Headed League(lead 확인; Bank-Note 1893이 뒤라 방향은 Bank-Note → RHL) / r:million-pound-bank-note-red-headed-league-1(case speculative, 3쌍) — claim "Both tales hand a poor man in London rich men's money for next to nothing, picking him out by his looks, as a stake in a game he cannot see. (쌍: 'as one may see by your face' ↔ 'I cannot recall when I have seen anything so fine.'; 'They’ve got a game, or a scheme…—let it go.' ↔ 'some great hoax or fraud, though what its object might be I could not imagine.'; 'I have a bet on you.' ↔ 'The £ 4 a week was a lure…playing for thousands?'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Study in Scarlet(Watson의 기록이 아직 없음, 공을 넘기는 신조 없음)·Scandal·Hound(권태 대사 없음)에서 거짓; ②는 Overcoat(집에 가서도 베낌, 사무실이 떠나지 않음)·Hard Times·Christmas Carol에서 거짓; ③은 Candide(신병 모집관이 키를 보고 돈을 줌 — 판돈도 '게임'의 자각도 없음)·Around the World(Fogg는 내기를 건 쪽)·Hadleyburg·The Bet에서 거짓. 내지 않은 것(task leads): A Study in Scarlet — 'the more bizarre … the less mysterious'는 'It is a mistake to confound strangeness with mystery'의 되풀이지만 Rue Morgue('outré character of its features')·Hound('The more outré and grotesque')에도 있고, '설명하면 시시해진다'는 Scandal에도 있어 대체 테스트 실패 → heading:detection 공유로만. Iliad(Pope 역)·찬송가는 그래프 작품 아님 → gloss 없음.

Jane's dead uncle leaves her a fortune, which she shares openly with her cousins. Pip's comes from the living convict he once fed, and his one good use of it is secret." (쌍: 'Yes, Pip, dear boy, I’ve made a gentleman on you! It’s me wot has done it!' ↔ 'your uncle, Mr. Eyre of Madeira, is dead; that he has left you all his property, and that you are now rich'; 'she returned, again nodding steadily, “I let you go on.”' ↔ 'I said I was sorry for his disappointment, but Jane Eyre was dead'; 'The whole business was so cleverly managed, that Herbert had not the least suspicion of my hand being in it.' ↔ 'twenty thousand pounds, the sum in question, divided equally between the nephew and three nieces of our uncle'). ② note Great Expectations answers The Fall of the House of Usher / r:great-expectations-fall-of-the-house-of-usher-1 — claim "Both books bring a visitor into a shut, rotting house to divert its sick owner, and the house falls when that life ends. Roderick thinks the stones shaped his family for centuries, and the house sinks with its last two. Miss Havisham stopped the clocks herself; her house is auctioned for building materials." (쌍: 'I want diversion, and I have done with men and women. Play.' ↔ 'with a view of attempting, by the cheerfulness of my society, some alleviation of his malady'; 'she laid the whole place waste, as you have seen it, and she has never since looked upon the light of day' ↔ 'that silent, yet importunate and terrible influence which for centuries had moulded the destinies of his family'; 'The House itself was to be sold as old building materials, and pulled down.' ↔ 'the deep and dank tarn at my feet closed sullenly and silently over the fragments of the “House of Usher.”'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Oliver Twist(아버지 유언을 숨긴 건 이복형, 나누지 않음)·Sign of the Four(Mary Morstan의 익명 진주는 런던의 Thaddeus, 유언·숨기는 여자 없음)·Kim(라마가 학비를 드러내 놓고 냄)·Wuthering Heights(Heathcliff는 해외에서 부자가 되지만 고아를 부자로 만들지 않음)에서 거짓, 반대쪽은 죄수 후원자와 스스로 숨은 후원자가 되는 고아가 Great Expectations에만 있어 거짓; ②는 House of the Seven Gables(Phoebe가 갇힌 Clifford를 위로하지만 집은 서 있고 집안은 이어짐)·Haunted and the Haunters(산 사람의 의지, 방 하나만 헐라는 권고)·Jane Eyre(Thornfield는 타지만 Jane은 고용된 가정교사, Rochester는 삶)·Turn of the Screw·Canterville·Yellow Wallpaper에서 거짓, 반대쪽은 시계를 스스로 멈춘 주인이 Great Expectations에만 있어 거짓. 실패한 lead 없음. 내지 않은 것: Oliver Twist(같은 작가, lead 아님 — 'brought up by hand'와 교구 고아, 구절 쌍 없음 → leads), Hamlet·Richard III·Collins's Ode·George Barnwell·'the Eastern story'·Herbert의 'land of the Arabian Nights'(그래프 작품 아니거나 이야기를 가리키지 않음 → gloss 없음), Dickens의 1861 결말 변경 편지와 Forster 주 [270]의 첫 결말(편지 날짜 없음, 결말은 편지·서문·에세이가 아님 → 두 번째 context 후보로 leads), David Copperfield(편지에 이름이 나오나 아직 그래프 작품 아님 → G37-04 몫). content/authors/charles-dickens.md는 이미 Great Expectations 항목이 있어 고치지 않음. site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

우리 원문의 절 번호 'Ii·Iii·Iv' 표기 그대로). 새 heading:drunk-on-the-war-path(맑을 땐 순하다는 마을 술꾼이 '전쟁길'에 나서 큰길에서 원수 하나를 불러냄) — index enacts: 이 작품(Scratchy Wilson), Huckleberry Finn(Boggs). note 3·reading 3(모두 answers, contact none, 대체 테스트 통과 — `catalog.py substitute` 제안 Tom Sawyer·Sleepy Hollow·Huckleberry Finn·Open Boat도 검토해 note에 기록): ① bride → huckleberry-finn answers(r:bride-comes-to-yellow-sky-huckleberry-finn-1, strong) — claim: 두 마을 모두 순하다는 술꾼이 '전쟁길'로 큰길에서 원수를 불러내는데, Twain의 군중은 비웃고 맑은 정신의 대령이 딸이 달려오는 앞에서 Boggs를 쏘며, Crane의 마을은 숨고 신부가 거기 있다는 것만으로 총 한 발 없이 싸움이 끝난다. ② bride → last-of-the-mohicans answers(r:bride-comes-to-yellow-sky-last-of-the-mohicans-1, moderate) — claim: 둘 다 옛 싸움꾼의 '마지막'으로 변경을 끝내는데, Cooper의 Magua는 Cora 앞에서 망설이지만 살육은 이어지고 Uncas가 죽어 Tamenund가 종족을 애도하며, Crane의 '옛 패거리의 마지막'은 신부 앞에서 권총을 내리고 집에 간다 — 학살이 아니라 결혼으로 끝나는 변경. ③ bride → outcasts-of-poker-flat answers(r:bride-comes-to-yellow-sky-outcasts-of-poker-flat-1, moderate) — claim: 둘 다 교회 같은 정적 속에 결혼하러 가는 순박한 한 쌍을 총잡이들 사이에 들이는데, Harte의 추방자들은 연인을 웃음에서 감싸고 도박사는 제 심장에 총을 쏘며, Crane은 한 쌍을 짐꾼의 농담에 맡기고 총잡이는 권총 둘을 집어넣고 살아서 돌아간다. 판정: leads의 'rewrites parody'(Mohicans·Outcasts)는 기각 — 원문이 두 작품의 장면·인물·이름을 하나도 가져오지 않고, Scratchy의 'Apache scalp music'·'war-trail'은 서부 활극 일반을 비꼬는 것이라 다른 인디언 활극·광산촌 이야기로 바꿔도 참(대체 테스트 실패); 구절 쌍이 성립하는 부분만 answers로 냄. 내지 않음: Luck of Roaring Camp(아기가 몇 달에 걸쳐 캠프를 바꿈 — 결혼으로 멈춘 총싸움과 짝지을 구절 없음), Open Boat(같은 작가·같은 책일 뿐 공유 장면·질문 없음), Reluctant Dragon(heading:the-arranged-fight는 미리 짠 싸움 — Scratchy의 싸움은 짜지 않았고 취소됨). gloss 없음(술집 허풍담·'Sunday school'·'Apache scalp music'은 라이브러리 작품이 아님). context 없음: Starrett의 Men, Women and Boats 서론(PG 7239, work:starrett-crane-estimate)은 이 작품을 언급하지 않음 — 1898년 서평이나 Crane 편지가 있으면 context 후보(leads).

Carton sends Darnay out under his own name and stays to die. The Pimpernel drives the cart out himself, dressed as a knitting hag, and goes home to rescue again." (쌍: 'some say it’s the plague! If it is, I sha’n’t be allowed to come into Paris to-morrow.' ↔ 'Apparently the English advocate is in a swoon?'; '…“tricotteuses,” … who sat there and knitted, whilst head after head fell beneath the knife' ↔ 'the knitting-women … count One.'; 'And all for sheer sport and devilry of course!—saving men, women and children from death' ↔ 'It is a far, far better thing that I do, than I have ever done'). ② note The Man Who Was Thursday answers The Scarlet Pimpernel(Thursday 1908이 뒤라 from Thursday) / r:man-who-was-thursday-scarlet-pimpernel-1 — claim "Both books hide dangerous men in parts that look harmless: Orczy's brainless fop and loathed Jew, Chesterton's open ranter and palsied professor. (쌍: 'Why, then, dress up as an anarchist, you fool!' ↔ 'The mask of the inane fop had been a good one, and the part consummately well played.'; 'I acted the paralytic Professor so well, that now I can’t leave off.' ↔ 'wearing already that mask of somnolent indifference which you have never laid aside until . . . until now.'; 'I am the man in the dark room, who made you all policemen.' ↔ 'the soldiers took no notice of the Jew, who had driven Citoyen Chauvelin to this spot.'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 뷰에 공포정치 파리가 Tale뿐(Two Brothers·Jekyll은 분신·옷 바꾸기뿐, 성문·뜨개질 여자·사형수 없음), 반대쪽 Kim·Thirty-Nine Steps·Thursday엔 단두대에서 구하는 일이 없어 거짓; ②는 Secret Agent(Verloc은 양쪽에 있으나 무해한 배역·사냥꾼 조종 없음)·Thirty-Nine Steps(도로공 변장, 추적자를 몰지 않음)·Blue Cross(순박해 보이는 신부는 도망자 아님), 반대쪽 Kim·Scandal in Bohemia(변장은 한때, 친구 앞에서도 벗지 못하는 배역·추적자를 고용한 도망자 없음)에서 거짓. 실패한 lead 없음. 내지 않은 것: Thirty-Nine Steps·Kim(변장 일반, 구절 쌍 없음), 바보 가면 heading(뷰에 같은 짝 없음 → leads). (쌍: 'The walls were carefully sounded…' ↔ 'The police have laid bare the floors, the ceilings, and the masonry of the walls…'; 'It was so small that a rat could hardly pass through.' ↔ 'will not admit, throughout their extent, the body of a large cat.'; 'causing it to turn upon its master at the other side' ↔ 'bore still in mind the dreaded whip, was instantly converted into fear'). ② note Rikki-Tikki-Tavi answers The Adventure of the Speckled Band(새로 찾은 후보; Rikki 1894가 뒤라 방향은 Rikki → SB) / r:rikki-tikki-tavi-speckled-band-1(case moderate, 3쌍) — claim "In both, a deadly Indian snake comes at night through a small hole in the wall into a room where people sleep, and the defender sits silent in the dark until it moves. Doyle's adder is sent by a man and kills him; Kipling's cobra comes by its own plan to empty the house, and dies there." (쌍: 'a brick pulled out to make a sluice for the bath water' ↔ 'the rope was there as a bridge for something passing through the hole…'; 'Rikki-tikki stayed still as death.' ↔ 'we waited in absolute darkness.'; 'Those who kill snakes get killed by snakes' ↔ 'Violence does, in truth, recoil upon the violent…'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Sign of the Four(잠긴 방·굴뚝 질문은 같으나 Tonga는 사람, 지붕 뚫린 문, 채찍·주인 없음)·Styles·Rikki에서 거짓, SB 쪽은 Hound(봉인된 방 없음)·Moonstone에서 거짓; ②는 Kaa's Hunting·Hound·Rue Morgue에서 거짓. 내지 않은 것: Sign of the Four(같은 작가) — heading:sealed-room 공유뿐, 짐승·주인·채찍 대응 없음. Hound of the Baskervilles — 짐승을 상속자에게 보내는 계략은 같으나 봉인된 방·구멍·자기 짐승에 죽는 주인 없음. Rue Morgue·Rikki-Tikki-Tavi·Sign of the Four 노드 leads에 한 줄씩 더함. site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

(쌍: 'The light showed a pale, square-jawed face with keen eyes' ↔ 'Over the edge there peeped a clean-cut, boyish face, which looked keenly about it'; 'a little white scar near his right eyebrow' ↔ 'Has a white splash of acid upon his forehead.'; 'Somehow I couldn’t do it myself…plain clothes man to do the job.' ↔ 'I have had one or two little scores of my own to settle with Mr. John Clay'). ② note After Twenty Years answers Rip Van Winkle(새로 찾은 후보) / r:after-twenty-years-rip-van-winkle-1(case moderate, 3쌍) — claim "Both bring a man back after twenty years to where the old eating-house of his youth stood, now replaced, asking after an old friend. (쌍: 'there used to be a restaurant where this store stands' ↔ 'the village inn—but it too was gone.'; 'If Jimmy is alive on earth he’ll be here by that time.' ↔ 'Nicholas Vedder? why, he is dead and gone these eighteen years!'; '“You’re not Jimmy Wells,” he snapped.' ↔ 'I’m not myself—I’m somebody else'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Dracula(이마의 붉은 흉터로 백작을 알아보나 경찰도 옛 인연도 없음)·Treasure Island(Billy Bones의 흰 칼자국, 잠복·체포 없음)·Blue Cross(Flambeau를 키로 알아봄)에서 거짓; ②는 Great Expectations(Satis House가 헐렸으나 옛 친구를 묻지도 정체를 의심하지도 않음)·Tale of Two Cities·Connecticut Yankee에서 거짓. 내지 않은 것: The Two Brothers('just like two brothers'는 비유일 뿐, 구절 쌍 없음), The Necklace(몇 해 뒤 만난 친구의 'How you are changed!'와 반전 결말 — 반전 결말 공유는 A21에서 heading으로), heading:sleeper-wakes(Bob을 아무도 못 알아보는 게 아님)·detection(풀 수수께끼 없음)에 index 안 함. Chekhov's pair dream apart, read each other's greed in a glance, and hate each other until a wrong number ends it." 기존 work leads에 줄 추가: million-pound-bank-note, the-bet, hadleyburg. 내지 않은 후보(leads에 이유): Vanka(같은 작가지만 맞는 장면·질문·구절 쌍 없음), Necklace(로와젤 부부의 빚은 10년 갚는 실제 빚이고 부부가 서로 미워하는 구절과 맞는 쌍 없음), heading:money-as-test(부자·낯선 이가 판돈을 내놓지 않음, 표는 아내 것)·wish-granted-to-ruin(소원이 이뤄지지 않음)은 index 안 함, Bank-Note는 새 heading에 넣지 않음(벌 월급 이야기지 상금을 공상으로 쓰지 않음).

Mathilde asks four hundred francs for her own gown, borrows diamonds that prove paste and pays ten years for one night. Mrs. Sommers drops her children's list for silk at $1.98, and the story ends before any reckoning." (쌍: 'really absorbed in speculation and calculation' ↔ 'She reflected several seconds, making her calculations and wondering also what sum she could ask'; 'She smiled, just as if she had been asked to inspect a tiara of diamonds…' ↔ 'Suddenly she discovered, in a black satin box, a superb diamond necklace…'; 'It was like a dream ended.' ↔ 'All was ended for her.'). 대체 테스트: Necklace → Bank-Note(아내 아님·돈을 안 씀)·Madame Bovary(몇 달의 빚)·Paul's Case(훔친 돈, 소년)면 거짓 → 통과. contact none(Chopin의 Maupassant 번역은 T0). ② note Pair of Silk Stockings answers The Story of an Hour (같은 작가) / r:pair-of-silk-stockings-story-of-an-hour-1 (case moderate) — claim "Chopin twice gives a hemmed-in wife a stretch of time to herself. Louise, freed by news of a death, sees clearly, claims years ahead and prays that life be long. Mrs. Sommers, freed by fifteen dollars, does not think at all; she has one afternoon and a cable car she wishes would never stop." (쌍: 'A vision of the future like some dim, gaunt monster sometimes appalled her…' ↔ 'It was only yesterday she had thought with a shudder that life might be long.'; 'She was not going through any acute mental process or reasoning with herself' ↔ 'A clear and exalted perception enabled her to dismiss the suggestion as trivial.'; 'a powerful longing that the cable car would never stop anywhere…' ↔ 'She breathed a quick prayer that life might be long.'). 대체 테스트: Hour → The Awakening(몇 달과 바다, 한 시간의 기도 없음)·Yellow Wallpaper(오래 살기를 바랄 자유 없음)면 거짓 → 통과. contact none(refers/read만 계산). ③ note Paul's Case answers Pair of Silk Stockings / r:pauls-case-pair-of-silk-stockings-1 (case moderate) — claim "Both spend money not meant for pleasure on new clothes, a fine dinner and a show among rich strangers who take them for their own. Paul's money is stolen, and when 'the play was over' he jumps. Mrs. Sommers's is her children's; she rides home, wishing only that the car never stop." (쌍: 'His new street suit he put on in the fitting-room' ↔ 'Here, in a retired corner, she exchanged her cotton stockings for the new silk ones…'; 'On the part of the hotel management, Paul excited no suspicion.' ↔ 'When she entered her appearance created no surprise, no consternation…'; 'the sinking sensation that the play was over' ↔ 'The play was over, the music ceased, the crowd filed out.'). 대체 테스트: Stockings → Necklace(식사·연극 없고 모두가 알아봄)·Bank-Note(외상으로 입음)면 거짓 → 통과. contact none(Cather는 Awakening을 서평했으나 이 단편을 읽은 기록 없음). 각 reading의 tried는 begin 뷰의 소설·단편 131편(병렬로 병합된 After Twenty Years·Speckled Band 포함 — 둘 다 claim 거짓). 내지 않은 후보: The Awakening(같은 작가 — Edna의 자유는 몇 달이고 바다로 끝나 오후 하루의 지출과 짝지을 구절 없음, 같은 작가 answers는 구절이 더 가까운 Hour가 맡음), £1,000,000 Bank-Note(heading:money-as-test — Henry는 지폐가 부르는 외상으로 먹고 입으며 사람들이 돈에 절함; Mrs. Sommers는 작은 것을 현금으로 사고 아무도 그녀의 돈을 모름), Madame Bovary(Lheureux에게 진 몇 달의 빚과 파멸 — 하루 오후의 스타킹·장화·장갑과 짝지을 구절 없음).

I had been in a nightmare.'). gloss 없음(Prometheus·Tantalus·Ixion·Ollendorff·Davy Jones·Hermes는 라이브러리 작품 아님). context 없음: PG 22246에 Publishers' Note(Putnam 1894, 'originally written for two Christmas annuals')가 있으나 저자가 회사라 person으로 기록할 수 없음 → leads. 승인용 note·reading(contact 모두 none, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·단편 140편 중 자기 뺀 139편 → 모두 passed): ① note The Upper Berth answers The Signal-Man(task lead 확인) / r:upper-berth-signal-man-1(case moderate, 3쌍) — claim "Both set a man of sense against a haunting that has already cost lives, and he argues nerves, fancy and coincidence. Dickens's narrator never sees the spectre, and his reasons outlast the signalman. Crawford's Brisbane spends his reasons on the porthole, keeps watch with the captain, and holds the drowned thing in his arms." (쌍: Welsh rare-bit ↔ 'deception of his sense of sight'; 'either imagination or something supernatural' ↔ 'My eyes were on the bell'; 'disagreeable coincidence of several suicides' ↔ 'a remarkable coincidence'). ② note The Upper Berth answers A Descent into the Maelström(task lead 확인, case speculative) / r:upper-berth-descent-into-the-maelstrom-1 — claim "Both sea survivors tell a night of terror to listeners and point to their bodies as proof. Poe's fisherman studies the whirl, reads its law in the wreckage, and escapes by it, expecting no belief. Brisbane studies the porthole, finds no law in it, and ends his tale unsure himself of what he held." (쌍: 'I watched it intently' ↔ 'present observation'; 멍 ↔ 흰 머리; 'if it was a ghost' ↔ 'they did not believe it'). ③ note 'Oh, Whistle, and I'll Come to You, My Lad' answers The Upper Berth(task lead 아님; James 1904가 뒤) / r:whistle-and-ill-come-upper-berth-1(case moderate) — claim "Both hauntings come from the unused bed in a sceptic's room. Brisbane blames the steward, loses the port with the captain, and grapples a drowned body that breaks his arm." (쌍: 흐트러진 빈 침대를 하인 탓 ↔ 침대를 안 정리한 스튜어드 탓; 창틀과 씨름 ↔ 포트 고리와 씨름; 이불로 된 몸 ↔ 익사체). ④ note The Open Window answers The Upper Berth(task lead 아님; 같은 웨이브 앞 단계 G25-02, Saki 1914가 뒤) / r:open-window-upper-berth-1(case moderate) — claim "Both keep an opening for men lost and never found, and a man bolts from the room. Crawford's port will not stay shut, the drowned come back through it, and those who run die. Saki's window is open in a girl's tale, the lost walk in muddy and alive, and the runner is a nervous guest." (쌍: 'window is kept open every evening' ↔ 'nobody on board as can keep this 'ere port shut'; 'bodies were never recovered' ↔ 'they were not found'; 'bolted out' ↔ 'They bolt out of bed'). 실패한 후보: 없음(task leads 둘 다 확인). 내지 않은 것: Haunted and the Haunters(밤샘 감시만 겹치고 그건 ①·③이 담음), heading:yarn-and-its-hearer(Brisbane은 35세, 틀이 이야기 뒤에 돌아오지 않음)·haunted-house·sealed-room·out-of-the-whirlpool에 index 안 함. (쌍: 거울 앞 'She looked at her reflection in the mirror long, carefully, and critically.' ↔ 'was lost in ecstasy at her reflection in the mirror'; 남편의 희생 'I sold the watch to get the money to buy your combs.' ↔ 'he was laying aside just that amount to buy a gun'; 결말 '…most unwisely sacrificed for each other the greatest treasures of their house' ↔ 'Why, my necklace was paste!…'). ② note Gift of the Magi answers A Christmas Carol(새 후보) / r:gift-of-the-magi-christmas-carol-1 — claim "Both keep Christmas in a household on a small weekly wage that the narrator puns into the husband's name, the husband going without a greatcoat. (쌍: 'contracting to a modest and unassuming D.' ↔ 'Bob had but fifteen "Bob" a-week himself'; 'He needed a new overcoat and he was without gloves.' ↔ '(for he boasted no great-coat)'; 'Eight dollars a week or a million a year—what is the difference?' ↔ 'as well as golden goblets would have done'). ③ note Gift of the Magi answers The Little Mermaid(새 후보) / r:gift-of-the-magi-little-mermaid-1 — claim "Both give a girl's long hair, the beauty she wraps herself in, to a woman who trades in it for love's sake, and what it buys goes unused. (쌍: 'made itself almost a garment for her.' ↔ 'she wrapped herself in her long, thick hair.'; '“Will you buy my hair?” asked Della.' ↔ 'We have given our hair to the witch'; 'let’s put our Christmas presents away and keep ’em a while.' ↔ 'then she flung it far away from her into the waves'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Million Pound Bank-Note(빌린 것, 희생 없음)·Overcoat(아내 없음, 자기 외투)·Madame Bovary에서 거짓; ②는 Silas Marner·Great Expectations·Oh, Whistle에서 거짓; ③은 Fisherman and His Soul(칼은 쓰임, 머리카락 없음)·Light Princess·Marsh King's Daughter에서 거짓. 내지 않은 것: Million Pound Bank-Note(대체 테스트 실패 — Necklace도 맞음), Overcoat(외투 없는 한 줄뿐, 짝 구절 없음); heading:borrowed-riches·the-witchs-price에 index 안 함. - [x] G25-04 2026-10-09 — The Phantom Rickshaw 입고(모두 proposed), `catalog.py check --stage G25-04` 0 errors 0 warnings, `test_catalog.py --stage G25-04` 통과. work:phantom-rickshaw(story, person:rudyard-kipling 기존; title·author ev = PG 2806 'Title: The Phantom 'Rickshaw, and Other Ghost Stories'·'Author: Rudyard Kipling'; first_pub 1885는 서가 연도, Quartette 1885는 T0 → leads), text:phantom-rickshaw-pg2806(PG 오독 'leaning hack'·'come hack'은 Wheeler 스캔에서 'back' — leads에만, 원문 안 고침). index enacts heading:sceptic-haunted('Fancy the ghost of a hillman!' · 'So there were ghosts of ‘rickshaws after all…'). context: 기존 work:kipling-phantom-rickshaw-preface → phantom-rickshaw, role preface(ev 'one man insisted upon dying because he believed himself to be haunted'; locus 'he preferred to die; vowing at the last that he was hag-ridden.'). gloss 없음(Evening Hymn·Hamlet·Tennyson The Princess·Inferno는 그래프 작품 아님). 승인용 note·reading(모두 case moderate, contact none, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·단편 150편 → passed): ① note The Phantom Rickshaw answers The Signal-Man / r:phantom-rickshaw-signal-man-1 — claim "Both tales post an apparition on the haunted man's way, crying the same words, while a sceptic blames the eye. Dickens's spectre warns of disaster on the line; Kipling's cries a dead woman's plea for forgiveness to the man who wronged her, and its one warning, at a landslip, only saves him for more haunting." (쌍: 'Jack! Jack dear! Please forgive me' ↔ 'Below there! Look out! Look out!'; 'Therefore, I conclude that it’s Eyes.' ↔ 'disease of the delicate nerves that minister to the functions of the eye'; 'the ‘rickshaw came to a dead stop under … shale cliff' ↔ 'Within six hours after the Appearance, the memorable accident'). ② note The Turn of the Screw answers The Phantom Rickshaw(James 1898이 나중 작품이라 James → Kipling 방향) / r:turn-of-the-screw-phantom-rickshaw-1 — claim "Both tales have the one who sees a dead woman force a loved companion to look at her, and lose the companion for it. Pansay, begging Kitty to speak to the ghost, confesses the wrong that raised it; James's governess accuses Flora of seeing and hiding, and the child, seeing nothing, begs to be taken away." (쌍: 'Where on earth do you see anything?' ↔ 'I can’t see them anywhere.'; 'there, there, there, and you see her as well as you see me!' ↔ 'Come and look at It.'; 'Take me away … from her!' ↔ 'Thank you, Mr. Pansay … that’s quite enough.'). ③ note The Man Who Would Be King shares-form(frame) The Phantom Rickshaw(같은 작가 lead; King 1888이 나중) / r:man-who-would-be-king-phantom-rickshaw-1 — claim "Kipling frames both tales alike: the narrator asks a broken man for his story 'from beginning to end', the man clings to his own name, and an official blames sun or overwork for his death. The Rickshaw opens with that verdict and its narrator rejects it; the King closes on it." (쌍: 'Tell me all you can recollect of everything from beginning to end.' ↔ 'write out the whole affair from beginning to end'; 'I am Peachey—Peachey Taliaferro Carnehan' ↔ 'I’m Jack Pansay on leave at Simla—at Simla!'; 'He was admitted suffering from sun-stroke.' ↔ 'Overwork started his illness … and killed him'). answers가 아니라 shares-form인 이유: 두 이야기가 같은 질문에 답하는 게 아니라 무너진 사람의 이야기를 받아 적는 Kipling의 틀을 공유함. 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 A Christmas Carol(Scrooge의 위장 탓 이론은 있으나 재앙의 장소·되풀이되는 외침 없음)·Haunted and the Haunters·Oh, Whistle(외침·눈 진단 없음)에서 거짓; ②는 Signal-Man(화자도 보지 못하나 연인이 아니고 떠나지 않음)·Yellow Wallpaper·Canterville·Usher에서 거짓; ③은 Frankenstein(Walton이 청하지 않음, 공식 사인 없음)·Heart of Darkness·Lord Jim(Marlow가 말함)·Jekyll(발견된 진술)에서 거짓. 내지 않은 것: A Christmas Carol(과제 밖 후보 — 'disorder of the stomach' ↔ 'Eyes, Brain, and Stomach', 잘못에서 온 유령이지만 Marley는 피해자가 아니라 같은 죄인; 뒤에 볼 lead로 leads에 적음), Dracula('the dead travel fast'는 둘 다 Bürger의 Lenore에서 온 속담이고 Dracula가 나중 작품, 장면 없음). 상대 작품 노드(Signal-Man·Turn of the Screw·Man Who Would Be King)는 고치지 않음. site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

(쌍: 'He was standing at the end of the wall where there was a tree.' ↔ 'An old, old ivy vine … climbed half way up the brick wall.'; '…he would get his death in the rain. Henry'; first_pub 1904는 서가 연도, 첫 지면은 T0 → leads), text:furnished-room-pg(The Four Million, PG 2776, 판본 표기 없음). 새 heading:the-let-room-and-its-past('The let room and its past' — 세 든 방에 새로 든 이가 그 방의 자국·손상·냄새를 앞서 산 이들의 흔적으로 읽고, 그들에게 일어난 일이 새 세입자에게 미침) — index enacts: furnished-room('One by one, as the characters of a cryptograph become explicit, the little signs left by the furnished room’s procession of guests developed a significance.' · 'There be many people will rayjict the rentin’ of a room if they be tould a suicide has been after dyin’ in the bed of it.'), yellow-wallpaper('Else, why should it be let so cheaply? (쌍: 'And he breathed the breath of the house—a dank savour…' ↔ 'an atmosphere which had no affinity with the air of heaven…'; 'It seemed incredible that all this malice and injury had been wrought upon the room…' ↔ 'that silent, yet importunate and terrible influence which for centuries had moulded the destinies of his family'; 'Once again he answered loudly: “Yes, dear!”…' ↔ 'Madman! I tell you that she now stands without the door!') ② note The Gift of the Magi answers The Furnished Room(lead '같은 작가' 확인; Magi 1905가 뒤라 Magi에서 나감) / r:gift-of-the-magi-furnished-room-1 — claim "O. Henry twice sets young love in a cheap New York furnished room with a pier glass between the windows, and a woman's hair. Jim looks about for Della's sold hair as she stands before him; the lodger hunts for Eloise, finds a common hair-bow, and dies never told she died there." (쌍: 'There was a pier glass between the windows of the room…' ↔ 'a foot-wide cheap pier glass between the two windows'; 'Jim looked about the room curiously.' ↔ 'he sprang to wrest from it a token…'; 'Beautiful combs… just the shade to wear in the beautiful vanished hair.' ↔ 'But the black satin hair-bow also is… common ornament, and tells no tales.') ③ note The Furnished Room answers The Yellow Wallpaper(새 후보) / r:furnished-room-yellow-wallpaper-1 — claim "Both put a newcomer in a let room whose damaged walls tell of earlier occupants, and a woman seems to be within the room. (쌍: 'Tiny finger prints on the wall spoke of little prisoners…' ↔ 'for the windows are barred for little children…'; 'the resentful rage at false household gods…' ↔ 'they must have had perseverance as well as hatred.'; 'unable to perceive that she was there beside, around, against, within, above him' ↔ 'And it is like a woman stooping down and creeping about behind that pattern.'). 대체 테스트에서 따져 본 것: ①은 Haunted and the Haunters(연인 없음)·Wuthering Heights(Heathcliff는 Catherine의 옛 방에서 죽지만 집이 썩지 않고 아무도 생매장하지 않음)·Yellow Wallpaper(아무도 죽지 않음)·Cask of Amontillado에서 거짓; ②는 Last Leaf(체경·연인·머리카락 없음)·Cop and the Anthem·After Twenty Years·Necklace(파리, 잃은 것은 다이아몬드)에서 거짓; ③은 Haunted and the Haunters(세입자의 증오로 상한 벽 없음)·Usher(세 든 방 아님)·Turn of the Screw·Northanger Abbey(손님 방, 손상 없음)에서 거짓. 실패한 후보: 없음(leads 둘 다 구절 쌍과 대체 테스트로 확인). 내지 않은 것: Haunted and the Haunters(짝 구절 없음, 집의 공포는 마술사의 의지), Last Leaf(같은 작가, 배경만 같고 짝 구절 없음); heading:haunted-house에 index 안 함(범위가 가족의 집 — 하숙집엔 가족 없음). 상대 작품 노드는 고치지 않음(leads는 work:furnished-room에 적음). site 코드 변경 없음(sw 그대로).

Wisdom·Marr Murray 역, PG 913; 번역 연도 없음 → leads). 새 heading:the-dead-mans-papers('The dead man's papers' — 편집자가 죽은 젊은이의 편지·일기를 펴내며 서문에서 그를 어떻게 읽을지 말함) — index enacts: hero-of-our-time('I LEARNED not long ago that Pechorin had died on his way back from Persia.' 외 1), sorrows-of-young-werther('I have carefully collected whatever I have been able to learn of the story of poor Werther, and here present it to you'). context: work:lermontov-hero-preface(1841 재판 작가 서문, PG 913 부록에서 전사 → content/contexts/lermontov-hero-preface.txt) → hero-of-our-time, role preface, ev 'he is a composite portrait, made up of all the vices which flourish, fullgrown, amongst the present generation'. gloss 없음(본문의 Byron·Rousseau Confessions·Goethe의 Mignon·Pushkin·Scott은 라이브러리 작품 아님). 승인용 note·reading(둘 다 answers, close-reading, contact none, case moderate, 각 3쌍, 대체 테스트 begin 뷰 소설·단편 151편 → passed): ① note A Hero of Our Time answers The Sorrows of Young Werther(task lead 확인) / r:hero-of-our-time-sorrows-of-young-werther-1 — claim "Both print a dead young man's own papers under an editor's preface and weigh his despair against a pistol. (쌍: 'The news afforded me great delight; it gave me the right to print these notes' ↔ 'To his spirit and character you cannot refuse your admiration and love: to his fate you will not deny your tears.'; 'not that despair which is cured at the muzzle of a pistol, …' -2 ↔ 'It is not despair: it is conviction that I have filled up the measure of my sufferings'; 'To none has my love brought happiness, because I have never sacrificed anything for the sake of those I have loved' -2 ↔ 'One of us three must die: it shall be Werther.'). ② note Notes from Underground answers A Hero of Our Time(task lead '쓸모없는 사람' 확인; Hero 1840이 앞이라 Notes → Hero) / r:notes-from-underground-hero-of-our-time-1 — claim "Both measure the man of their generation against a novel's hero, call him a cripple, and set him against an officer over a point of honour. (쌍: 'a novel needs a hero, and all the traits for an anti-hero are expressly gathered together here' -2 ↔ 'His aim is to make himself the hero of a novel.' -2; 'we are all divorced from life, we are all cripples, every one of us, more or less' -2 ↔ 'I became a moral cripple.' -2; 'Oh, if only that officer had been one of the sort who would consent to fight a duel!' -1 ↔ 'I shall have the honour to send my second to you to-day' -2). Notes는 'Lermontov’s Masquerade'만 부름(라이브러리 작품 아님 → refers 없음, leads). 실패·제외 후보: shares-form(Hero → Werther, 편집자 틀)은 answers 쌍 하나와 heading으로 흡수; heading:double은 둘째 자아가 없어 색인 안 함; Madame Bovary·Awakening·Jekyll은 Werther 자리 대체 테스트에서 탈락(편집자 서문·권총 없음).

(쌍: 'The old-timer had been very serious in laying down the law that no man must travel alone …' ↔ 'The bottom’s likely to drop out at any moment. …'; 'The dog did not want to go. (쌍: 'It did not lead him to meditate upon his frailty as a creature of temperature …' ↔ 'When it occurs to a man that nature does not regard him as important …'; 'If he had only had a trail-mate he would have been in no danger now. …' ↔ 'Then he touched a man in the bottom of the boat, and called his name.'; 'A good idea, he thought, to sleep off to death. Bazarov says yes: men are frogs on hind legs, a rectified society will cure moral sickness, and all else may be denied. The Underground Man denies the one thing Bazarov keeps, the laws of nature, and says men act against their advantage to prove they are not piano-keys." 실패·보류한 후보: 없음(두 lead 모두 note).
